- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Textual Steering Vectors Can Improve Visual Understanding in Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14071v1)
- **Authors**: Woody Haosheng Gan, Deqing Fu, Julian Asilis, Ollie Liu, Dani Yogatama, Vatsal Sharan, Robin Jia, Willie Neiswanger
- **Abstract**: Steering methods have emerged as effective and targeted tools for guiding large language models' (LLMs) behavior without modifying their parameters. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs), however, do not currently enjoy the same suite of techniques, due in part to their recency and architectural diversity. Inspired by this gap, we investigate whether MLLMs can be steered using vectors derived from their text-only LLM backbone, via sparse autoencoders (SAEs), mean shift, and linear probing. We find that text-derived steering consistently enhances multimodal accuracy across diverse MLLM architectures and visual tasks. In particular, mean shift boosts spatial relationship accuracy on CV-Bench by up to +7.3% and counting accuracy by up to +3.3%, outperforming prompting and exhibiting strong generalization to out-of-distribution datasets. These results highlight textual steering vectors as a powerful, efficient mechanism for enhancing grounding in MLLMs with minimal additional data collection and computational overhead.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of textual steering vectors to improve the visual understanding capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The core idea is to leverage existing techniques for steering text-only LLMs by extracting steering vectors from their text backbones and applying these vectors to the hidden states of the MLLMs. The authors investigate three methods for extracting steering vectors: sparse autoencoders (SAEs), mean shift, and linear probing. The approach is evaluated on various visual reasoning tasks, showing that textual steering consistently enhances multimodal accuracy, often outperforming traditional prompting methods and exhibiting some generalization to out-of-distribution datasets.  A key finding is that mean shift often outperforms SAEs in this context.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The novelty of this paper is moderate. While the idea of steering LLMs isn't new, its application to MLLMs, specifically using steering vectors derived *solely* from the text-only backbone is a valuable insight. Previous work has explored cross-modal steering, but often relying on paired multimodal data. Repurposing existing language steering techniques for the multimodal setting represents a practical contribution. The finding that mean shift outperforms SAEs in this setting is also a valuable, though incremental, contribution, consistent with existing literature in steering on unimodal models.

**Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons:

*   **Accessibility:** It introduces a relatively simple and efficient method for improving MLLMs. It doesn't require retraining or fine-tuning the entire model, which is beneficial in terms of computational resources and time.
*   **Interpretability:**  Steering vectors offer a degree of interpretability, allowing researchers to understand what aspects of the model's internal representation are being modified.
*   **Generalizability:** Demonstrating out-of-distribution generalization strengthens the argument that the improvements aren't simply due to overfitting to the training data.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly addresses the gap in steering techniques for MLLMs.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The method is clearly described and relatively easy to implement.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The authors provide extensive experimental results across multiple models, tasks, and datasets.
*   **Ablation Studies:**  The investigation of different intervention tokens (text, image, both) sheds light on how information is being processed in the MLLM.
*   **Reproducibility:** Using openly-available models and common datasets increases the chances of reproducible results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Text Backbone:** The method's efficacy depends on the existence and quality of a text-only LLM backbone, which limits its applicability to certain MLLM architectures. Further exploration of steering vectors derived "directly" from MLLMs themselves, without the necessity of a backbone, would be a good improvement.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the improvements are consistent, they aren't necessarily revolutionary. In some cases, the gains are modest.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach, it lacks a deep theoretical explanation of why textual steering vectors work for MLLMs. Exploring questions of the interplay between visual and textual embeddings in the intermediate layers, and relating that structure to the empirical results, would strenghten the paper.
*   **Lack of Novelty (to a degree):** Prior work has extensively examined steering in language-only models. While the cross-modal transfer is a valuable contribution, the basic steering techniques are not novel.
*   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** The description of the grid search for hyperparameters could be more thorough, and how the training set was divided for grid search and subsequent evaluation for determining improvement.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging researchers to explore lightweight steering techniques for MLLMs. The simplicity of the approach could make it a valuable tool for improving the performance of existing models without requiring extensive retraining. It also provides a foundation for further research into the relationship between visual and textual representations in MLLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.  While the paper presents a valuable and practical approach to steering MLLMs, its novelty is somewhat limited by its reliance on pre-existing steering techniques for text-only LLMs and the incremental improvement over prompting alone. The empirical validation is thorough, and the results are promising, but the lack of a strong theoretical underpinning and the relatively small size of some improvements prevents a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CE-LSLM: Efficient Large-Small Language Model Inference and Communication via Cloud-Edge Collaboration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14085v1)
- **Authors**: Pengyan Zhu, Tingting Yang
- **Abstract**: Emerging intelligent service scenarios in 6G communication impose stringent requirements for low latency, high reliability, and privacy preservation. Generative large language models (LLMs) are gradually becoming key enablers for the integration of semantic communication and computation. However, due to the limited computational resources of edge devices and the increasing complexity of heterogeneous terminal access, existing centralized inference approaches fail to meet the dual demands of response efficiency and data privacy in edge-side inference tasks. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a novel collaborative inference architecture that integrates cloud-based LLMs with edge-deployed small language models (SLMs), enabling dynamic scheduling and sharing of semantic-level intermediate states, and establishing a unified computation-communication paradigm tailored for 6G networks. Specifically, a key-value (KV) cache reuse mechanism is introduced to enhance the semantic understanding of edge models through contextual guidance from the cloud, while significantly reducing edge-side computational and storage overhead. Furthermore, a cross-node parallel scheduling mechanism is proposed to achieve asynchronous coordination between model state loading and decoding computation, thereby improving edge responsiveness. In addition, we investigate layer alignment and representation compression strategies between heterogeneous models to alleviate the communication burden on the edge. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed architecture exhibits superior adaptability and scalability in terms of inference latency, system stability, and concurrent processing capacity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CE-LSLM: Efficient Large-Small Language Model Inference and Communication via Cloud-Edge Collaboration" proposes a novel architecture for performing large language model (LLM) inference in resource-constrained edge environments.  The key idea is to combine a cloud-based LLM with edge-deployed smaller language models (SLMs) in a way that offloads complex tasks requiring high computational power to the cloud while enabling faster, privacy-preserving local inference on edge devices. The architecture employs several optimization techniques including:

*   **Key-Value (KV) cache reuse:**  The edge models reuse KV caches pre-computed by the cloud LLM to enhance inference accuracy with minimal edge-side computation.
*   **Cross-node parallel scheduling:** Overlapping model loading and decoding computations.
*   **Layer matching and representation compression:** Strategies to reduce the communication overhead between the cloud and edge.

The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that the proposed approach reduces latency, enhances system stability and improves concurrent processing capacity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of cloud-edge collaboration for LLM inference is not entirely new. However, the paper makes a decent attempt at integrating several existing techniques (KV cache reuse, model compression, parallel scheduling) within a unified framework specifically designed for 6G network scenarios. The explicit focus on 6G requirements (low latency, privacy) and the emphasis on collaborative *edge-edge* KV cache sharing (although the details are quite shallow) increase the paper's novelty. The exploration of layer-wise similarity based on representation similarity matrices (RSM) and Centered Kernel Alignment (CKA) is a notable addition, even though the application is standard.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant and crucial problem: how to deploy and efficiently use large language models in resource-constrained environments, particularly in emerging 6G networks. If successful, the approach could have a tangible impact on the deployment of intelligent services in various applications (industrial automation, smart healthcare, etc.).  The improvements demonstrated in terms of inference latency, system stability, and throughput are promising.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly defines the problem and motivates the need for a cloud-edge collaborative inference architecture.
    *   The architectural design is well-explained, and the key optimization techniques are described in detail.
    *   The experimental results are compelling and demonstrate the advantages of the proposed approach over baseline methods.
    *   The paper provides a strong and comprehensive analysis of related work, properly positioning CE-LSLM within existing research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Edge-Edge KV sharing details are shallow:**  The description of how edge devices effectively share cached KV data is high-level and could benefit from deeper elaboration. The claim of sharing is unsubstantiated.
    *   **Limited Practicality:**  The experiments, while convincing, are conducted in a controlled lab environment and do not fully capture the complexities of real-world 6G deployments (e.g., fluctuating bandwidth, heterogeneous devices, security considerations).
    *   **Lack of Implementation Details:** Critical details regarding the implementation (software platform, communication protocols, specific model configurations) are missing, making it challenging to reproduce the results.
    *   **Missing comparisons:** While the authors consider vLLM and naive cloud/edge baselines, they could have compared CE-LSLM to more recent and relevant works in edge-based LLM inference or collaborative inference architectures. Comparing with CE-COLLM in [16] is good, but needs to be more exhaustive.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in cloud-edge collaborative AI, particularly in the context of 6G networks.  The ideas presented could inspire the development of more efficient and scalable architectures for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. It reinforces the need for careful consideration of data transmission, model compression, and task scheduling.

*   **Justification for Score:** Despite the identified weaknesses, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field. It combines several existing techniques in a novel way to address a practical problem, and the experimental results are encouraging. However, the lack of implementation details, limited evaluation in realistic 6G scenarios, and missing comparisons prevent it from achieving a higher score.
    There is an adequate evaluation, but more diverse comparisons could be done.
    Also, the paper would benefit from detailing potential limitations of CE-LSLM in adverse edge network conditions, like intermittent connectivity, and discussing failure handling scenarios.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Beyond Chains: Bridging Large Language Models and Knowledge Bases in Complex Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14099v1)
- **Authors**: Yihua Zhu, Qianying Liu, Akiko Aizawa, Hidetoshi Shimodaira
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Base Question Answering (KBQA) aims to answer natural language questions using structured knowledge from KBs. While LLM-only approaches offer generalization, they suffer from outdated knowledge, hallucinations, and lack of transparency. Chain-based KG-RAG methods address these issues by incorporating external KBs, but are limited to simple chain-structured questions due to the absence of planning and logical structuring. Inspired by semantic parsing methods, we propose PDRR: a four-stage framework consisting of Predict, Decompose, Retrieve, and Reason. Our method first predicts the question type and decomposes the question into structured triples. Then retrieves relevant information from KBs and guides the LLM as an agent to reason over and complete the decomposed triples. Experimental results demonstrate that PDRR consistently outperforms existing methods across various LLM backbones and achieves superior performance on both chain-structured and non-chain complex questions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Beyond Chains: Bridging Large Language Models and Knowledge Bases in Complex Question Answering."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PDRR (Predict-Decompose-Retrieve-Reason), a novel framework for Knowledge Base Question Answering (KBQA) that aims to overcome the limitations of existing chain-based KG-RAG (Knowledge Graph Retrieval-Augmented Generation) methods. Chain-based methods often struggle with complex questions that require more sophisticated logical structures beyond simple sequential hops. PDRR addresses this by incorporating a planning module inspired by semantic parsing methods. It first *Predicts* the question type (chain or parallel), then *Decomposes* the question into structured triples representing sub-tasks.  The framework then *Retrieves* relevant information from the knowledge base and *Reasons* over the retrieved triples using a Large Language Model (LLM).  This structured approach enables PDRR to handle more complex questions and improve reasoning transparency. The authors evaluate PDRR on the ComplexWebQuestions (CWQ) and WebQSP datasets, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing approaches, especially on questions requiring more complex logic. The paper emphasizes that PDRR is training-free, making it more practical and accessible.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the structured planning module that combines LLM reasoning with elements inspired by semantic parsing. While KG-RAG is a well-established area, the *explicit* integration of a planning stage to guide the LLM's reasoning *over* the knowledge graph and break it into sub-tasks is a meaningful contribution. Other planning-based methods exist, but many involve training LLMs, which this approach avoids. The parallel question decomposition addresses an important shortcoming of chain-based methods. However, the individual components (LLM reasoning, KG retrieval) are not novel in themselves; the novelty lies in the specific combination and control mechanism.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in KBQA: the inability of chain-based KG-RAG methods to handle complex, multi-faceted questions. By improving the logical structuring of questions and incorporating parallel reasoning strategies, PDRR enhances both the accuracy and the transparency of KBQA systems.  The empirical results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing training-free methods, suggesting that PDRR provides a valuable step toward more robust and reliable KBQA systems.  The training-free nature of the method is a major strength, increasing its accessibility and practicality. However, it would benefit from comparisons against more fully-trained competitive models as a stronger point of reference.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined framework with explicit planning and controlled LLM reasoning.
    *   Significant improvements in performance over training-free KG-RAG baselines, particularly on complex questions.
    *   Training-free approach, increasing practicality.
    *   Detailed ablation studies and analysis of question structures.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty lies primarily in the integration of existing techniques, rather than a fundamentally new approach to KG-RAG.
    *   The analysis in some sections of the paper (e.g., Ablation Study) lacks sufficient depth.
    *   The framework performs slightly underperforms ToG on WebQSP due to occasional errors in question type prediction. The paper doesn't go into enough detail as to why this happens.
    *   The paper would benefit from comparison against more fully trained competitive models as a stronger point of reference, despite the advantages of a training-free approach.
    *   Limitations in handling highly complex question structures are acknowledged, pointing to a need for more sophisticated hybrid datasets and methods, but could have been further explored in the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** PDRR has the potential to influence future research in KBQA by promoting the use of structured planning and controlled reasoning.  It could also serve as a starting point for developing more sophisticated hybrid methods that combine the benefits of LLMs with the precision and transparency of knowledge graphs.

* **Rigorous Rationale:** Overall, the paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field. PDRR is an effective framework for KBQA that bridges LLMs and KBs by incorporating a planning module. The gains in accuracy, especially on complex question types, demonstrate the value of the structured reasoning approach, and the training-free nature of the method significantly enhances its appeal. However, limitations are present and it would benefit from more thorough analysis in some sections.

### MultiHal: Multilingual Dataset for Knowledge-Graph Grounded Evaluation of LLM Hallucinations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14101v1)
- **Authors**: Ernests Lavrinovics, Russa Biswas, Katja Hose, Johannes Bjerva
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have inherent limitations of faithfulness and factuality, commonly referred to as hallucinations. Several benchmarks have been developed that provide a test bed for factuality evaluation within the context of English-centric datasets, while relying on supplementary informative context like web links or text passages but ignoring the available structured factual resources. To this end, Knowledge Graphs (KGs) have been identified as a useful aid for hallucination mitigation, as they provide a structured way to represent the facts about entities and their relations with minimal linguistic overhead. We bridge the lack of KG paths and multilinguality for factual language modeling within the existing hallucination evaluation benchmarks and propose a KG-based multilingual, multihop benchmark called \textbf{MultiHal} framed for generative text evaluation. As part of our data collection pipeline, we mined 140k KG-paths from open-domain KGs, from which we pruned noisy KG-paths, curating a high-quality subset of 25.9k. Our baseline evaluation shows an absolute scale increase by approximately 0.12 to 0.36 points for the semantic similarity score in KG-RAG over vanilla QA across multiple languages and multiple models, demonstrating the potential of KG integration. We anticipate MultiHal will foster future research towards several graph-based hallucination mitigation and fact-checking tasks.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "MultiHal: Multilingual Dataset for Knowledge-Graph Grounded Evaluation of LLM Hallucinations."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MultiHal, a new multilingual, multi-hop benchmark designed for evaluating factual consistency (hallucinations) in large language models (LLMs). Existing benchmarks are often English-centric and rely on unstructured context (web links, text passages), neglecting structured factual resources like Knowledge Graphs (KGs). MultiHal addresses these limitations by: (1) Curating a dataset of over 25k high-quality question-answer pairs sourced from seven existing hallucination benchmarks. (2) Linking these question-answer pairs to KG paths extracted from Wikidata, providing structured factual grounding. (3) Offering high-quality translations of the data into five languages (German, Spanish, French, Italian, Portuguese). (4) Evaluating the KG path quality with LLM-as-a-judge (GPT-40 Mini) and (5) Demonstrating through baseline experiments (vanilla QA vs. KG-RAG) the potential of KG integration to improve semantic similarity between model predictions and ground truth. The dataset and code are publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Strengths:*

*   **Addresses a Critical Gap:** The paper effectively identifies and addresses a crucial gap in the LLM evaluation landscape: the lack of multilingual benchmarks that leverage structured knowledge for hallucination assessment. The focus on KGs as a source of ground truth is timely and relevant.
*   **Multilingual and Multi-Hop:** The multilingual aspect is a significant strength, as it enables evaluation of LLMs across different languages, uncovering potential language-specific biases in factuality. The multi-hop nature adds complexity, challenging models to reason across multiple relationships within the KG.
*   **Data Curation Pipeline:** The paper presents a well-defined and scalable framework for linking question-answer pairs to a KG, involving entity linking, KG querying, and LLM-based path quality evaluation. The use of LLM-as-a-judge to filter noisy KG paths is a novel approach.
*   **Public Availability:** Making the dataset and code publicly available is crucial for reproducibility and encourages further research in this area.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Reliance on LLM-as-a-Judge:** The quality of the KG paths hinges on the LLM-as-a-judge (GPT-40 Mini) which is based on rating each path individually, and could inject its own biases and potentially limit the dataset's objectivity. The correlation with semantic similarity is decent, but not exceptionally high, suggesting some limitations in the LLM's ability to accurately assess path relevance. Table 3 shows the Spearman correlation for the GPT 40-Mini is just 0.485, which indicates it will not perform as well at removing all the noisy KGs.
*   **Limited Baseline Evaluation:** The baseline experiments demonstrate the potential of KG integration, but they are relatively simple (prompt-based KG-RAG). A more comprehensive evaluation including fine-tuning or adapter-based approaches would strengthen the claims. Although the authors demonstrate some mean score improvements (0.12-0.36) across several languages, there isn't any formal statistical significance, which would make the results more concrete.
*   **Potential for Dataset Bias:** While sourced from existing benchmarks, the MultiHal dataset might inherit some biases present in those datasets.
*   **Simplicity of Knowledge Injection** The knowledge injection method is common yet relatively simple (P= (K, Q). To advance the project further, knowledge injection should be applied to advanced knowledge techniques such as reasoning or graph editing.

*Novelty and Significance:*

The novelty lies in the combination of several aspects: (1) multilingual benchmark with KG-based factual grounding, (2) scalable pipeline linking Q\&A pairs to KGs, and (3) leveraging LLM-as-a-judge for KG path filtering.

The significance is that MultiHal provides a valuable resource for: (1) Evaluating the factual consistency of LLMs in a multilingual setting, (2) Exploring different knowledge injection methods, and (3) Developing techniques for hallucination mitigation using structured knowledge. However, the dependence on LLM-as-a-judge is a risk for future scalability.

*Justification for Score:*

I am assigning a score of **7** out of 10.

*   The paper addresses a very relevant problem and introduces a useful resource for the community. The multilingual aspect and the focus on KGs are significant strengths.
*   However, the reliance on LLM-as-a-judge for KG path filtering introduces a potential for bias and limits scalability. The baseline experiments are also relatively basic.
*   Despite these limitations, MultiHal has the potential to stimulate further research in this important area, and its public availability is a major plus.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Legal Rule Induction: Towards Generalizable Principle Discovery from Analogous Judicial Precedents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14104v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Fan, Tianshi Zheng, Yiran Hu, Zheye Deng, Weiqi Wang, Baixuan Xu, Chunyang Li, Haoran Li, Weixing Shen, Yangqiu Song
- **Abstract**: Legal rules encompass not only codified statutes but also implicit adjudicatory principles derived from precedents that contain discretionary norms, social morality, and policy. While computational legal research has advanced in applying established rules to cases, inducing legal rules from judicial decisions remains understudied, constrained by limitations in model inference efficacy and symbolic reasoning capability. The advent of Large Language Models (LLMs) offers unprecedented opportunities for automating the extraction of such latent principles, yet progress is stymied by the absence of formal task definitions, benchmark datasets, and methodologies. To address this gap, we formalize Legal Rule Induction (LRI) as the task of deriving concise, generalizable doctrinal rules from sets of analogous precedents, distilling their shared preconditions, normative behaviors, and legal consequences. We introduce the first LRI benchmark, comprising 5,121 case sets (38,088 Chinese cases in total) for model tuning and 216 expert-annotated gold test sets. Experimental results reveal that: 1) State-of-the-art LLMs struggle with over-generalization and hallucination; 2) Training on our dataset markedly enhances LLMs capabilities in capturing nuanced rule patterns across similar cases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Legal Rule Induction: Towards Generalizable Principle Discovery from Analogous Judicial Precedents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Legal Rule Induction (LRI) task, which involves deriving concise, generalizable doctrinal rules from sets of analogous judicial precedents.  It formalizes the task, defines legal rules using a three-element structure (hypothetical condition, behavioral pattern, legal consequence), and presents a novel benchmark dataset. This dataset consists of two parts: LRI-AUTO, a large-scale dataset automatically curated from Chinese court judgments for model tuning, and LRI-GOLD, a meticulously curated test set annotated by legal experts.  The paper then evaluates the performance of various Large Language Models (LLMs) on this benchmark, highlighting the challenges they face (e.g., over-generalization, hallucination) and demonstrating that fine-tuning on LRI-AUTO can significantly improve LLMs' ability to capture nuanced rule patterns. It further explores different inductive reasoning pipelines, including Direct Induction, Chain-of-Thought (CoT), Long-CoT, and a novel iterative induction-verification pipeline called SILVER.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a substantial contribution by formally defining the Legal Rule Induction (LRI) task, which is a critical yet understudied area within computational law. Most existing research focuses on rule application, while rule discovery from precedents has been largely unexplored computationally. The paper's emphasis on *induction* rather than mere *deduction* or similarity matching is a significant departure from the norm and aligns well with actual legal reasoning. The formal definition and the three-element structure offer a valuable framework.
*   **Significance:** The creation and release of the LRI dataset (both LRI-AUTO and LRI-GOLD) are extremely valuable. The lack of suitable benchmarks has hindered progress in this area, and this dataset provides a much-needed resource for training and evaluating models. By using civil law precedents which often cite the statutes applied, the authors were able to more easily align cases to potential underlying rules, which in turn enabled the creation of a much larger training dataset than would otherwise be possible.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper provides a well-defined problem statement and clearly articulates the challenges involved in legal rule induction.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The dataset creation process is described in detail, with clear explanations of the data sources, preprocessing steps, and annotation protocols. The use of both automated and expert-annotated datasets is a strong point.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are comprehensive, evaluating a range of LLMs and inductive reasoning pipelines.
    *   **Important Findings:** The findings are insightful, revealing the limitations of current LLMs in LRI and demonstrating the benefits of fine-tuning. The analysis of explicit vs. implicit rules provides a nuanced understanding of the task.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The dataset is based on Chinese law, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other legal systems. The three-element structure may also require adaptation depending on jurisdiction.
    *   **Automated Metrics:** While the LLM-as-a-Judge approach is interesting, the reliability of using an LLM for evaluation should be considered with caution. Manual evaluation, while costly, is essential to truly validate the accuracy and usefulness of the induced rules. Although the authors did perform a manual quality audit of LRI-AUTO sets, they did not mention doing the same to evaluate the LLM judgments of the induced rules, which should have been the focus of the audit.
    *   **Complexity of Real-World Legal Reasoning:**  The three-element model of rules, while useful, is a simplification of the messy and often ambiguous nature of real-world legal reasoning.  There are many legal concepts and nuances beyond simple if/then logic that this framework would struggle to capture.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of computational law by:
    *   Encouraging further research on legal rule induction.
    *   Providing a benchmark for evaluating and comparing different approaches.
    *   Motivating the development of more sophisticated models capable of capturing the complexities of legal reasoning.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign this paper a score of **8**.

The paper's formalization of the LRI task and the creation of the LRI dataset represent a significant advance. The experimental results are compelling, and the findings offer valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities of using LLMs for legal rule induction. The limitations related to generalizability and automated evaluation are acknowledged and do not substantially detract from the paper's overall contribution. Addressing the limitations regarding automated evaluation metrics would increase the paper's impact and overall robustness. Despite these weaknesses, this paper offers a significant contribution to the field of AI and Law.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Personalized Conversational Benchmark: Towards Simulating Personalized Conversations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14106v1)
- **Authors**: Li Li, Peilin Cai, Ryan A. Rossi, Franck Dernoncourt, Branislav Kveton, Junda Wu, Tong Yu, Linxin Song, Tiankai Yang, Yuehan Qin, Nesreen K. Ahmed, Samyadeep Basu, Subhojyoti Mukherjee, Ruiyi Zhang, Zhengmian Hu, Bo Ni, Yuxiao Zhou, Zichao Wang, Yue Huang, Yu Wang, Xiangliang Zhang, Philip S. Yu, Xiyang Hu, Yue Zhao
- **Abstract**: We present PersonaConvBench, a large-scale benchmark for evaluating personalized reasoning and generation in multi-turn conversations with large language models (LLMs). Unlike existing work that focuses on either personalization or conversational structure in isolation, PersonaConvBench integrates both, offering three core tasks: sentence classification, impact regression, and user-centric text generation across ten diverse Reddit-based domains. This design enables systematic analysis of how personalized conversational context shapes LLM outputs in realistic multi-user scenarios. We benchmark several commercial and open-source LLMs under a unified prompting setup and observe that incorporating personalized history yields substantial performance improvements, including a 198 percent relative gain over the best non-conversational baseline in sentiment classification. By releasing PersonaConvBench with evaluations and code, we aim to support research on LLMs that adapt to individual styles, track long-term context, and produce contextually rich, engaging responses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PERSONACONVBENCH, a new large-scale benchmark designed for evaluating personalized reasoning and generation within multi-turn conversations using Large Language Models (LLMs). The benchmark uniquely integrates both personalization and conversational structure, offering three core tasks: sentence classification, impact regression, and user-centric text generation across 10 diverse Reddit-based domains. The authors benchmark several commercial and open-source LLMs and demonstrate that incorporating personalized conversational history leads to substantial performance improvements compared to non-personalized baselines. The benchmark and associated code are released to facilitate research on LLMs capable of adapting to individual conversational styles, tracking long-term context, and generating more contextually rich responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *combined* focus on both personalization and multi-turn conversational structure within a unified benchmark. While personalization and conversational modeling are each independently active research areas, their tight integration in PERSONACONVBENCH addresses a significant gap. Existing personalization benchmarks often treat each user utterance as independent, and multi-turn conversation work is often user-agnostic. PERSONACONVBENCH directly tackles the challenge of modeling user behavior *within* the context of ongoing conversations. This is a step up from previous work on single-turn personalized tasks.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the benchmark's potential to drive progress in developing more human-like and engaging conversational AI systems. The release of a large-scale, diverse dataset with well-defined tasks will likely spur further research into:

    *   Adaptive LLMs that tailor responses to individual users' communication styles and preferences.
    *   Better models for long-term context tracking within conversations.
    *   More nuanced and personalized response generation.
    *   Analyzing the effects of different levels of user interactivity.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The benchmark covers diverse Reddit domains, includes multiple evaluation paradigms (classification, regression, generation), and uses a unified prompting setup for fair comparisons.
    *   **Realistic Multi-User Setting:**  Moving beyond dyadic (user-agent) dialogues to a multi-user setting provides a more realistic and challenging environment for LLMs.
    *   **Clear Formalism:** The formal definitions of message representation, conversational graphs, trajectories, and user trajectory sets provide a solid framework for analysis.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate a clear benefit from incorporating personalized conversational history across several models.
    *   **Open Source Release:**  Availability of the dataset and code promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reddit-centric Data:** The Reddit-based data may not generalize perfectly to other conversational settings (e.g., customer service, virtual assistants) due to differences in communication styles and user demographics. The specific dataset construction methods and filtering criteria could introduce biases. The reliance on upvotes as an impact metric, while common, is also subject to popularity bias.
    *   **Limited Task Diversity:**  While the core tasks are well-defined, expanding the range of tasks could make the benchmark even more comprehensive. For example, adding tasks focused on dialogue coherence, topic maintenance, or user intent understanding could be beneficial.
    *   **Limited LLM Investigation:** While several strong models were investigated, there are other powerful models that could have been tried as well.
    *   **No fine-tuning evaluation**. All evaluations were conducted in the zero-shot setting.

*   **Potential Influence:** PERSONACONVBENCH has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating personalized conversational AI. It can guide the development of new algorithms and model architectures that effectively capture and leverage user-specific information within dynamic conversation contexts. The benchmark could also facilitate the analysis of existing LLMs' personalization capabilities and help identify areas for improvement.

* **Score:** 8

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid contribution with a novel and useful benchmark that addresses an important gap in personalized conversational AI research. The thorough experimental results demonstrate the value of the benchmark and provide a strong foundation for future work. The main weaknesses relate to the limited domain (Reddit), limited task diversity, and the need to evaluate the impact of various prompt and fine-tuning techniques on performance. However, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, solidifying its potential to significantly impact the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### DiagnosisArena: Benchmarking Diagnostic Reasoning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14107v1)
- **Authors**: Yakun Zhu, Zhongzhen Huang, Linjie Mu, Yutong Huang, Wei Nie, Shaoting Zhang, Pengfei Liu, Xiaofan Zhang
- **Abstract**: The emergence of groundbreaking large language models capable of performing complex reasoning tasks holds significant promise for addressing various scientific challenges, including those arising in complex clinical scenarios. To enable their safe and effective deployment in real-world healthcare settings, it is urgently necessary to benchmark the diagnostic capabilities of current models systematically. Given the limitations of existing medical benchmarks in evaluating advanced diagnostic reasoning, we present DiagnosisArena, a comprehensive and challenging benchmark designed to rigorously assess professional-level diagnostic competence. DiagnosisArena consists of 1,113 pairs of segmented patient cases and corresponding diagnoses, spanning 28 medical specialties, deriving from clinical case reports published in 10 top-tier medical journals. The benchmark is developed through a meticulous construction pipeline, involving multiple rounds of screening and review by both AI systems and human experts, with thorough checks conducted to prevent data leakage. Our study reveals that even the most advanced reasoning models, o3-mini, o1, and DeepSeek-R1, achieve only 45.82%, 31.09%, and 17.79% accuracy, respectively. This finding highlights a significant generalization bottleneck in current large language models when faced with clinical diagnostic reasoning challenges. Through DiagnosisArena, we aim to drive further advancements in AIs diagnostic reasoning capabilities, enabling more effective solutions for real-world clinical diagnostic challenges. We provide the benchmark and evaluation tools for further research and development https://github.com/SPIRAL-MED/DiagnosisArena.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DiagnosisArena: Benchmarking Diagnostic Reasoning for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiagnosisArena, a new benchmark specifically designed to rigorously assess the diagnostic reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in clinical scenarios. Unlike existing medical benchmarks which often rely on simplified multiple-choice questions or knowledge recall, DiagnosisArena presents complex, real-world clinical cases derived from top-tier medical journals. These cases consist of segmented patient data, including case information, physical examination results, and diagnostic tests, culminating in a final diagnosis.  The benchmark creation process involves a multi-stage curation pipeline: data collection from journals, data segmentation, iterative filtering by both AI and human experts, and expert-AI collaborative verification to ensure data quality and prevent leakage. The authors then evaluate the performance of various LLMs (both proprietary and open-source) on DiagnosisArena. Results reveal that even the most advanced reasoning models struggle to achieve high accuracy, highlighting a generalization bottleneck in their ability to handle complex clinical diagnostic challenges. The paper concludes that DiagnosisArena can drive further advancements in AI diagnostic reasoning capabilities, leading to more effective real-world healthcare solutions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Benchmark:** The paper addresses a clear gap in the existing landscape of medical LLM benchmarks. DiagnosisArena provides a more realistic and challenging evaluation environment, moving beyond basic knowledge recall to assess genuine diagnostic reasoning.
    *   **Rigorous Curation Process:** The multi-stage process used to construct the benchmark is a major strength. It ensures the quality, complexity, and diversity of the data, while actively mitigating data leakage and ambiguity. The inclusion of both AI and human experts in the curation process adds credibility and reduces potential biases.
    *   **Focus on Diagnostic Reasoning:** By focusing specifically on diagnosis (as opposed to treatment recommendations), the benchmark addresses a core aspect of clinical decision-making where AI could have a significant impact.
    *   **Emphasis on Real-World Cases:** The use of case reports from top-tier medical journals grounds the benchmark in actual clinical practice, enhancing its relevance.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper does a good job of structuring the pipeline and experiment with a fair amount of detail.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on GPT-40 for Evaluation:** Using GPT-40 as a judge introduces a potential bias, as the performance of the models might be influenced by GPT-40's own knowledge and reasoning patterns. While the paper attempts to mitigate this with categories like "relevant," the subjectivity remains.
    *   **Limited Generalizability of Case Reports:** While based on real-world cases, the complex and nuanced cases in top-tier journals might not fully represent the distribution of cases encountered in everyday clinical practice. This could limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Missing baseline details:** the paper did not mention the performance of human expert.
    *   **MCQ transformation:** I suggest dropping the MCQ version as it is a over simplified version and reduces the contribution, and can take the focus away from the true diagnosis.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The creation of DiagnosisArena itself represents a significant contribution to the field, given the limitations of existing medical benchmarks. Its focus on real-world complexity and rigorous diagnostic reasoning makes it a valuable tool for evaluating and improving the capabilities of medical LLMs.
    *   The findings from the initial LLM evaluations are also significant, highlighting the current gap between LLM performance and the requirements for professional-level medical diagnostics. This emphasizes the need for further research into improved reasoning models and data handling techniques in this domain.

*   **Potential Influence:** DiagnosisArena has the potential to become a standard benchmark for medical LLM research. It can drive innovation in AI diagnostic reasoning, encourage the development of more robust and reliable models, and ultimately contribute to the safe and effective deployment of AI in healthcare.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The benchmark has addressed an important point, as the current medical datasets are quite old.
    I feel that the rigorous analysis of each part of the curation has led to increased quality of the paper overall.
    However, the results, the dataset is extremely low compared to other medical benchmarks.
    I believe it could increase if you move away from MCQ and focus on the open world evaluations.

**Score: 7.5/10**

**Justification:**

DiagnosisArena addresses a critical need for more realistic and challenging benchmarks in the medical AI field. The rigorous curation process and focus on diagnostic reasoning are significant strengths. However, the reliance on GPT-40 for evaluation and limited generalizability of top-tier journal case reports are weaknesses. While it is a valuable tool for the LLM community to improve their medical model and benchmarking. The dataset is still very low to show significant contributions to the field. Overall, DiagnosisArena makes a valuable contribution with considerable potential, but there is room for improvement in the dataset, evaluation methods, and the model performance.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Invisible Entropy: Towards Safe and Efficient Low-Entropy LLM Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14112v1)
- **Authors**: Tianle Gu, Zongqi Wang, Kexin Huang, Yuanqi Yao, Xiangliang Zhang, Yujiu Yang, Xiuying Chen
- **Abstract**: Logit-based LLM watermarking traces and verifies AI-generated content by maintaining green and red token lists and increasing the likelihood of green tokens during generation. However, it fails in low-entropy scenarios, where predictable outputs make green token selection difficult without disrupting natural text flow. Existing approaches address this by assuming access to the original LLM to calculate entropy and selectively watermark high-entropy tokens. However, these methods face two major challenges: (1) high computational costs and detection delays due to reliance on the original LLM, and (2) potential risks of model leakage. To address these limitations, we propose Invisible Entropy (IE), a watermarking paradigm designed to enhance both safety and efficiency. Instead of relying on the original LLM, IE introduces a lightweight feature extractor and an entropy tagger to predict whether the entropy of the next token is high or low. Furthermore, based on theoretical analysis, we develop a threshold navigator that adaptively sets entropy thresholds. It identifies a threshold where the watermark ratio decreases as the green token count increases, enhancing the naturalness of the watermarked text and improving detection robustness. Experiments on HumanEval and MBPP datasets demonstrate that IE reduces parameter size by 99\% while achieving performance on par with state-of-the-art methods. Our work introduces a safe and efficient paradigm for low-entropy watermarking. https://github.com/Carol-gutianle/IE https://huggingface.co/datasets/Carol0110/IE-Tagger
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Invisible Entropy (IE)," a novel watermarking technique for large language models (LLMs) that addresses the challenges of low-entropy scenarios. Existing methods in such scenarios often require access to the original LLM for entropy calculation, posing security risks (model leakage) and computational cost issues. IE replaces the original LLM with a lightweight feature extractor and an entropy tagger to predict token entropy.  It also presents a "Threshold Navigator" that adaptively sets entropy thresholds to balance naturalness and detectability. Experiments on code generation tasks (HumanEval and MBPP) demonstrate IE's efficiency and comparable performance to state-of-the-art methods while significantly reducing parameter size. The paper emphasizes safety and efficiency improvements compared to existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in replacing the need for the original LLM during the watermark *detection* phase with a lightweight feature extractor and entropy tagger. This significantly improves safety by mitigating model leakage risks, a crucial concern in LLM deployment, and addresses the computationally expensive re-querying of the original LLM.  The introduction of the Threshold Navigator for adaptive entropy thresholding is also a valuable contribution, moving away from static, manually defined thresholds. The "Unified Feature Extractor" addresses the practical issue of different tokenizers across LLMs is valuable from a practical engineering standpoint.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is in providing a more practical and secure watermarking solution, especially for scenarios where the original LLM cannot be exposed for detection purposes. The efficiency gains in terms of parameter size and potentially detection time make it more deployable. The evaluation focuses on code generation, a relevant and challenging application for LLM watermarking, showcasing real-world utility.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a critical practical limitation:** IE directly tackles the model leakage and computational cost problems of existing entropy-based watermarking techniques.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper provides thorough experimental results on relevant datasets (HumanEval and MBPP) and compares IE with strong baselines, showing competitive performance.
    *   **Principled approach:** The Threshold Navigator is based on a theoretical analysis of watermark ratio and green token counts, providing a more adaptive and robust solution.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is generally well-structured and clearly explains the proposed method and its advantages.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Entropy Tagger Accuracy:** While the paper shows good overall performance, it acknowledges some performance decrease compared to precise entropy calculation using the original LLM. Further work is needed to improve the accuracy of the entropy tagger, potentially by focusing on specific low-entropy tokens.
    *   **Fixed Search Granularity:** The Threshold Navigator's fixed search granularity could limit optimization flexibility. Exploring adaptive search strategies would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Attack Robustness Assessment:** While the paper evaluates robustness to paraphrasing, more rigorous evaluations against various adversarial attacks (e.g., synonym substitution beyond variable names, token injection, character manipulation) would strengthen the results. The discussion of robustness is relatively limited. The attack robustness appears to be strong in the reported experiments, but only assesses paraphrasing attacks.
    *   **Generalizability:** The experiments are primarily focused on *code* generation. While code represents a low-entropy scenario and is a relevant application, demonstrating the approach's effectiveness on *natural language* tasks with varying degrees of entropy would improve confidence in its broader applicability. It's possible that the proposed method is more suited for code than for other text types.
*   **Potential Influence:** The IE framework has the potential to influence the design of more secure and efficient watermarking techniques. The idea of using a lightweight feature extractor and entropy tagger for watermark detection could be adopted in other LLM applications. The Threshold Navigator could inspire adaptive methods for balancing naturalness and detectability in watermarking.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practically relevant solution to the model leakage and computational cost issues associated with entropy-based watermarking. The adaptive thresholding mechanism is a significant step forward. However, the paper has limitations in entropy tagger accuracy, search granularity of the Threshold Navigator, evaluation of adversarial robustness (beyond simple paraphrasing), and demonstrated generalizability beyond code generation. The improvements in safety and efficiency justify a strong score, but the identified weaknesses prevent it from receiving an exceptionally high mark. While the paper makes a solid contribution, further work is needed to fully address its limitations and demonstrate its broader applicability and robustness.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Self-Reasoning Language Models: Unfold Hidden Reasoning Chains with Few Reasoning Catalyst
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14116v1)
- **Authors**: Hongru Wang, Deng Cai, Wanjun Zhong, Shijue Huang, Jeff Z. Pan, Zeming Liu, Kam-Fai Wong
- **Abstract**: Inference-time scaling has attracted much attention which significantly enhance the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in complex reasoning tasks by increasing the length of Chain-of-Thought. These longer intermediate reasoning rationales embody various meta-reasoning skills in human cognition, such as reflection and decomposition, being difficult to create and acquire. In this work, we introduce \textit{Self-Reasoning Language Model} (SRLM), where the model itself can synthesize longer CoT data and iteratively improve performance through self-training. By incorporating a few demonstration examples (i.e., 1,000 samples) on how to unfold hidden reasoning chains from existing responses, which act as a reasoning catalyst, we demonstrate that SRLM not only enhances the model's initial performance but also ensures more stable and consistent improvements in subsequent iterations. Our proposed SRLM achieves an average absolute improvement of more than $+2.5$ points across five reasoning tasks: MMLU, GSM8K, ARC-C, HellaSwag, and BBH on two backbone models. Moreover, it brings more improvements with more times of sampling during inference, such as absolute $+7.89$ average improvement with $64$ sampling times, revealing the in-depth, diverse and creative reasoning paths in SRLM against the strong baseline.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Self-Reasoning Language Models (SRLM), a method to improve LLMs through self-training. The core idea is to use a small amount of "reasoning catalyst" data (demonstrations of how to expand shorter CoTs) to guide the LLM in generating longer, more detailed reasoning chains for its own training data.  The SRLM iteratively refines its reasoning capabilities by generating rationales, selecting better ones, and using them to fine-tune the model. The paper explores various selection strategies (length, on-policy, off-policy) and evaluates the SRLM on several reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating improved performance and more stable training compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of self-reasoning and iterative refinement isn't entirely new.  Previous work has explored self-training and using LLMs to generate training data for themselves. However, the specific approach of using a *reasoning catalyst* to guide the expansion of reasoning chains is a distinguishable contribution.  The different selection strategies are also a nice, though not profoundly innovative, touch. The novelty lies in the combination of these elements and the focus on *general* instruction-following tasks where the answers aren't easily verifiable. While self-improving LLMs have been explored, this focuses on settings which previously self-improvement studies might have found infeasible to handle.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem: the scarcity of high-quality, long CoT data for instruction tuning. By enabling LLMs to self-generate and refine reasoning chains, the approach offers a way to scale up training data for complex reasoning tasks without relying heavily on human annotation or powerful, expensive models like GPT-4. The experiments demonstrate a tangible improvement in performance across several benchmarks, indicating the potential of this technique. The claim that smaller SRLMs can generate higher-quality data than GPT-4o for instruction-tuning is particularly significant. This could democratize access to better LLM training techniques. The investigation of different selection methods also gives valuable insights into how to control the self-improvement process. The results related to increasing sampling during inference, showing significant gains in performance, are compelling.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges associated with limited CoT data.
*   **Well-Defined Method:** The SRLM framework is well-defined, with clear steps for reasoning expansion, selection, and fine-tuning.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach across multiple benchmarks and base models. The ablation studies and analysis of sampling times provide valuable insights.
*   **Practical Implications:** The method is relatively simple to implement and could potentially be applied to a wider range of tasks.
* Good analysis of the relative benefits of different selectors and data sizes used in the catalyst.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Catalyst Data:** The experiments only use 1k or 5k samples for the catalyst, raising questions about scalability and the potential for bias introduced by the catalyst. It may be that the effectiveness is heavily sensitive to these 1-5k samples, meaning this will take time to properly bootstrap in real-world situations.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides limited theoretical analysis of why the reasoning catalyst works and how the different selection strategies affect training dynamics. The mechanism isn't entirely clear - is it simply providing good starting point, or are there other dynamics.
*   **Incremental Improvement:**  While the results are positive, the improvements are generally incremental rather than dramatic.
*   **Experiment Design:** Several potential elements of the experiment would benefit from additional clarification. What prompts were used for the generation of self-improved results and what filtering occurs with those samples? How are samples used for iterative training constructed from the data used in previous steps?

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence research in self-supervised learning for LLMs, instruction tuning, and scaling reasoning capabilities. The concept of a reasoning catalyst could inspire new methods for guiding LLM training and generating high-quality data.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a reasonably novel and practically useful method for improving LLMs through self-training. The reasoning catalyst is an interesting idea, and the experiments demonstrate improved performance. However, the incremental nature of the improvements, the limited scope of the catalyst data, and the lack of deep theoretical analysis temper the enthusiasm. While the contribution isn't groundbreaking, it is a valuable addition to the field and warrants further investigation. The relatively modest score reflects concerns about the limited novelty and potential scalability issues, as well as unclear experiment methodology.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MAS-KCL: Knowledge component graph structure learning with large language model-based agentic workflow
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14126v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan-Hao Jiang, Kezong Tang, Zi-Wei Chen, Yuang Wei, Tian-Yi Liu, Jiayi Wu
- **Abstract**: Knowledge components (KCs) are the fundamental units of knowledge in the field of education. A KC graph illustrates the relationships and dependencies between KCs. An accurate KC graph can assist educators in identifying the root causes of learners' poor performance on specific KCs, thereby enabling targeted instructional interventions. To achieve this, we have developed a KC graph structure learning algorithm, named MAS-KCL, which employs a multi-agent system driven by large language models for adaptive modification and optimization of the KC graph. Additionally, a bidirectional feedback mechanism is integrated into the algorithm, where AI agents leverage this mechanism to assess the value of edges within the KC graph and adjust the distribution of generation probabilities for different edges, thereby accelerating the efficiency of structure learning. We applied the proposed algorithm to 5 synthetic datasets and 4 real-world educational datasets, and experimental results validate its effectiveness in learning path recognition. By accurately identifying learners' learning paths, teachers are able to design more comprehensive learning plans, enabling learners to achieve their educational goals more effectively, thus promoting the sustainable development of education.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MAS-KCL, a novel algorithm for learning knowledge component (KC) graph structures. MAS-KCL leverages a multi-agent system (MAS) powered by large language models (LLMs) to iteratively refine KC graphs. The system employs a bidirectional feedback mechanism where AI agents evaluate edges within the KC graph and adjust generation probabilities, thereby optimizing structure learning. The authors validate MAS-KCL on synthetic and real-world educational datasets, demonstrating its effectiveness in identifying learning paths and assisting teachers in designing targeted interventions. The core idea is to inject LLM's reasoning capabilities into the graph structure learning process to better capture causal dependencies between KCs and improve learning path recognition.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper offers a few elements of novelty:

*   **Integration of LLMs in Graph Structure Learning:**  Applying LLMs to the task of KC graph structure learning within a multi-agent system is a relatively novel approach. Previous methods often relied on expert knowledge, data-driven conditional probabilities, or reinforcement learning, each with its own limitations.

*   **Agentic Workflow:** The proposed MAS-KCL algorithm uses agents (Positive Feedback, Negative Feedback, Game agent) to control the selection and weighting of edges in the KC Graph based on the LLM prompting.

*   **Bidirectional Feedback Mechanism:** The integration of positive and negative feedback agents guiding graph modification introduces a dynamic and adaptive learning approach.

**Significance:**

The potential significance lies in:

*   **Improved Learning Path Identification:** An accurate KC graph can help educators understand the dependencies between KCs, pinpoint areas where learners struggle, and create more effective interventions.

*   **Personalized Learning:** By understanding a learner's specific knowledge structure, it is possible to tailor learning plans to address their individual needs.

*   **Scalability and Interpretability:** The proposed approach using LLMs could potentially be more scalable and interpretable than traditional methods for KC graph learning. The integration of LLMs could enable the system to handle larger datasets and provide more intuitive explanations of the relationships between KCs.

**Weaknesses and Points for Critique:**

*   **LLM Reliance and Justification:** While the integration of LLMs is a core element, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth explanation of *why* LLMs are particularly well-suited for this task.  The paper briefly mentions their capability to generate personalized learning plans, but the specific advantages in capturing complex dependencies and causal relationships within the KC structure are not thoroughly explored. It's not immediately clear how an LLM understands the nuances of prerequisite relationships between specific knowledge components.

*   **Experimental Design and Baselines:** Although a few baseline algorithms are used, a stronger comparison would involve methods directly addressing the KC graph structure learning problem. The baseline algorithms used: Multi-Stage multi-objective Evolutionary Algorithm (MSEA), Golden Eagle Optimizer (GEO), Elite Evolution Strategy-based Harris Hawk Optimization algorithm (EESHHO), and Adaptive Geometry Estimation-based Many-Objective Evolutionary Algorithm II (AGE-MOEA-II) are multi-objective optimizers rather than methods for modeling KC structure.

*   **Dataset Characteristics:** A more detailed analysis of the properties of the real-world datasets and their potential impact on the performance of different algorithms is necessary. The choice of datasets and the size differences between the real-world examples could affect results.

*   **Scalability:** The computational cost of invoking LLMs repeatedly during the iterative graph refinement process is not addressed.  The practicality of MAS-KCL for very large KC structures or large learner populations needs further evaluation. While the paper mentions the computational resources used, the computational complexity is not thoroughly analyzed.

*   **Generalizability of LLMs:** Experiments need to be performed with other LLMs to show the generalizability of the proposed approach and rule out the LLM choice as a bias in the results.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the above analysis, the paper presents a novel concept with potential for real-world impact. However, several aspects require more rigorous investigation. The reliance on LLMs needs stronger justification and a clearer understanding of the mechanisms driving their performance. The experimental design could be improved with more relevant baselines and a more detailed dataset analysis.

Therefore, a score of **6** is assigned. This score reflects the novelty of the approach and the potential benefits in education but acknowledges the limitations in the current experimental validation and the need for a deeper theoretical understanding of how the LLM component contributes to improved performance.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Texts or Images? A Fine-grained Analysis on the Effectiveness of Input Representations and Models for Table Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14131v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zhou, Mohsen Mesgar, Heike Adel, Annemarie Friedrich
- **Abstract**: In table question answering (TQA), tables are encoded as either texts or images. Prior work suggests that passing images of tables to multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) performs comparably to or even better than using textual input with large language models (LLMs). However, the lack of controlled setups limits fine-grained distinctions between these approaches. In this paper, we conduct the first controlled study on the effectiveness of several combinations of table representations and models from two perspectives: question complexity and table size. We build a new benchmark based on existing TQA datasets. In a systematic analysis of seven pairs of MLLMs and LLMs, we find that the best combination of table representation and model varies across setups. We propose FRES, a method selecting table representations dynamically, and observe a 10% average performance improvement compared to using both representations indiscriminately.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of different input representations (text vs. images, and their combination) for table question answering (TQA) tasks using both large language models (LLMs) and multi-modal large language models (MLLMs). The authors build a new benchmark of 1600 instances from existing TQA datasets, controlling for question complexity (retrieval vs. reasoning) and table size (small vs. big). They systematically evaluate seven open-weight MLLMs and their corresponding LLM decoders. The study finds that the optimal combination of table representation and model varies across different settings, particularly with smaller models. The paper proposes FRES, a feature-based table representation selection method that dynamically chooses between text, image, or both representations, achieving improved performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its controlled, fine-grained analysis of input representations and model types in TQA.  While prior work has explored the effectiveness of images vs. text, this paper makes a significant contribution by introducing a controlled benchmark based on question complexity and table size, performing a systematic comparison across open-weight models. The FRES method, dynamically selecting representations, is a practical contribution that builds on the empirical findings.
*   **Significance:** The results offer valuable insights for practitioners working with TQA. Identifying that smaller models benefit from specific representation choices while larger models may not is an important practical finding. The FRES method provides a concrete way to improve TQA performance, especially for resource-constrained settings where smaller models are necessary. The release of the controlled benchmark is also a valuable contribution to the community, enabling more rigorous evaluation of TQA models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Controlled Experimental Design:**  The study's strength lies in its controlled variables. Question complexity and table size are well-defined and systematically varied, allowing for clear conclusions to be drawn.
    *   **Open-Weight Models:**  The exclusive use of open-weight models enhances reproducibility and allows for broader application of the findings.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper evaluates multiple models and conditions, providing a relatively comprehensive picture of the interplay between model architecture, input representation, and task characteristics.
    *   **Practical Contribution:** The FRES method is a straightforward technique that can be easily implemented and offers significant performance improvements.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Models:** While the study includes multiple models, the landscape of LLMs and MLLMs is vast. Exploring a wider range of architectures and sizes could further refine the findings.
    *   **Reliance on Existing Datasets:** The benchmark relies on existing TQA datasets, inheriting their biases and limitations. A truly novel dataset designed from the ground up with the study's goals in mind could be even more impactful.
    *   **Relatively Simple FRES Method:** While effective, the FRES method is somewhat simplistic. More sophisticated feature selection techniques or a learned model for representation selection could potentially yield even greater gains.
    *   **Lack of Detailed Ablation:** While the error analysis is insightful, more detailed ablation studies on the contribution of each component of the FRES approach could enhance the understanding of its effectiveness.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of TQA systems, particularly in settings where computational resources are limited. The FRES method can be readily adopted to improve the performance of existing models. The findings regarding the importance of table size and question complexity will inform future research on TQA. The open benchmark should encourage further development of TQA models and representation techniques.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper offers significant novel insights into TQA by conducting a controlled study that carefully considers various dimensions of the problem. The FRES method is a valuable practical contribution.  While there are some limitations in the scope of models and the sophistication of FRES, the paper is a solid contribution that advances the field. The practical value of the FRES method and the creation of a controlled benchmark elevate this work beyond a simple empirical study, justifying the score of 7. The score reflects that it is a valuable, well-executed study, but not a groundbreaking paradigm shift. It's a very good, incremental contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hunyuan-Game: Industrial-grade Intelligent Game Creation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14135v1)
- **Authors**: Ruihuang Li, Caijin Zhou, Shoujian Zheng, Jianxiang Lu, Jiabin Huang, Comi Chen, Junshu Tang, Guangzheng Xu, Jiale Tao, Hongmei Wang, Donghao Li, Wenqing Yu, Senbo Wang, Zhimin Li, Yetshuan Shi, Haoyu Yang, Yukun Wang, Wenxun Dai, Jiaqi Li, Linqing Wang, Qixun Wang, Zhiyong Xu, Yingfang Zhang, Jiangfeng Xiong, Weijie Kong, Chao Zhang, Hongxin Zhang, Qiaoling Zheng, Weiting Guo, Xinchi Deng, Yixuan Li, Renjia Wei, Yulin Jian, Duojun Huang, Xuhua Ren, Sihuan Lin, Yifu Sun, Yuan Zhou, Joey Wang, Qin Lin, Jingmiao Yu, Jihong Zhang, Caesar Zhong, Di Wang, Yuhong Liu, Linus, Jie Jiang, Longhuang Wu, Shuai Shao, Qinglin Lu
- **Abstract**: Intelligent game creation represents a transformative advancement in game development, utilizing generative artificial intelligence to dynamically generate and enhance game content. Despite notable progress in generative models, the comprehensive synthesis of high-quality game assets, including both images and videos, remains a challenging frontier. To create high-fidelity game content that simultaneously aligns with player preferences and significantly boosts designer efficiency, we present Hunyuan-Game, an innovative project designed to revolutionize intelligent game production. Hunyuan-Game encompasses two primary branches: image generation and video generation. The image generation component is built upon a vast dataset comprising billions of game images, leading to the development of a group of customized image generation models tailored for game scenarios: (1) General Text-to-Image Generation. (2) Game Visual Effects Generation, involving text-to-effect and reference image-based game visual effect generation. (3) Transparent Image Generation for characters, scenes, and game visual effects. (4) Game Character Generation based on sketches, black-and-white images, and white models. The video generation component is built upon a comprehensive dataset of millions of game and anime videos, leading to the development of five core algorithmic models, each targeting critical pain points in game development and having robust adaptation to diverse game video scenarios: (1) Image-to-Video Generation. (2) 360 A/T Pose Avatar Video Synthesis. (3) Dynamic Illustration Generation. (4) Generative Video Super-Resolution. (5) Interactive Game Video Generation. These image and video generation models not only exhibit high-level aesthetic expression but also deeply integrate domain-specific knowledge, establishing a systematic understanding of diverse game and anime art styles.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hunyuan-Game, a suite of generative AI models designed for professional-grade game asset creation. It consists of both image and video generation models. The image generation branch includes text-to-image, game visual effects generation (including text-to-effects and reference-based effects), transparent image generation, and game character generation. The video generation branch includes image-to-video generation, 360° A/T pose character video generation, dynamic illustration generation, video super-resolution, and interactive game video generation.  The models are trained on a massive dataset of game and anime images/videos. The paper claims state-of-the-art performance in visual fidelity and motion naturalness, particularly surpassing Midjourney, Kling, and Wan in game scenarios. The authors emphasize community-driven innovation and collaborative development as goals.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a large-scale project with potentially significant impact on the game development industry.  However, several aspects require careful scrutiny:

*   **Novelty:** While the paper claims several "industry-first" capabilities, the underlying techniques seem to be largely based on existing generative models (DiT, diffusion transformers, etc.) and fine-tuning them on game-specific data. The novelty lies primarily in the *scale* of the dataset, the *specific customizations* for game asset generation, and the *integration* of various generation tasks into a single suite. The prompt engineering and specific aesthetic quality scoring methods may also provide some incremental novelty.  However, the absence of detailed architectural descriptions and comparative ablation studies makes it difficult to fully assess the true algorithmic novelty. Are the modifications to the DiT architecture significant enough to warrant a high novelty score?

*   **Significance:** The significance of the project hinges on the claimed performance improvements and practical usability.
