### Towards Omnidirectional Reasoning with 360-R1: A Dataset, Benchmark, and GRPO-based Method
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14197v1)
- **Authors**: Xinshen Zhang, Zhen Ye, Xu Zheng
- **Abstract**: Omnidirectional images (ODIs), with their 360{\deg} field of view, provide unparalleled spatial awareness for immersive applications like augmented reality and embodied AI. However, the capability of existing multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) to comprehend and reason about such panoramic scenes remains underexplored. This paper addresses this gap by introducing OmniVQA, the first dataset and conducting the first benchmark for omnidirectional visual question answering. Our evaluation of state-of-the-art MLLMs reveals significant limitations in handling omnidirectional visual question answering, highlighting persistent challenges in object localization, feature extraction, and hallucination suppression within panoramic contexts. These results underscore the disconnect between current MLLM capabilities and the demands of omnidirectional visual understanding, which calls for dedicated architectural or training innovations tailored to 360{\deg} imagery. Building on the OmniVQA dataset and benchmark, we further introduce a rule-based reinforcement learning method, 360-R1, based on Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct. Concretely, we modify the group relative policy optimization (GRPO) by proposing three novel reward functions: (1) reasoning process similarity reward, (2) answer semantic accuracy reward, and (3) structured format compliance reward. Extensive experiments on our OmniVQA demonstrate the superiority of our proposed method in omnidirectional space (+6% improvement).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Towards Omnidirectional Reasoning with 360-R1: A Dataset, Benchmark, and GRPO-based Method":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the underexplored capability of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in understanding and reasoning about omnidirectional (360°) images (ODIs). To address this gap, the authors introduce OmniVQA, the first publicly available dataset and benchmark for omnidirectional Visual Question Answering (VQA). The OmniVQA dataset focuses on object localization, attribute analysis, and spatial relationship reasoning in panoramic scenes. The paper evaluates existing MLLMs on OmniVQA and reveals significant limitations in handling omnidirectional visual question answering. Furthermore, the authors propose 360-R1, a rule-based reinforcement learning method based on Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct, enhancing MLLMs' spatial reasoning and object identification abilities in 360° images. The 360-R1 method modifies group relative policy optimization (GRPO) with novel reward functions, including reasoning process similarity, answer semantic accuracy, and structured format compliance.  Experiments demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel dataset, OmniVQA, which is a significant contribution to the field. The lack of publicly available datasets for omnidirectional VQA has been a barrier to progress. Creating this dataset, focusing on specific challenges presented by 360° images (distortion, spatial reasoning), is a strong point. The rule-based reinforcement learning approach, 360-R1, while not radically different from existing RL techniques, has some new elements in its reward function design, making the reinforcement reward appropriate for panoramic images.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in several aspects. First, it highlights a crucial gap in MLLMs' capabilities – the understanding of 360° imagery, which is increasingly relevant with the growth of AR/VR/Embodied AI. Second, the OmniVQA dataset enables further research and development in this area. Third, 360-R1 provides a practical approach to improve MLLMs' performance on omnidirectional VQA, demonstrating improvements in spatial reasoning and object identification. Finally, carefully constructed reward functions that emphasize both reasoning quality and formatting consistency constitute an important contribution, highlighting the importance of both quality and structure in AI responses.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The OmniVQA dataset addresses a real need in the community. It's publicly available, which is a big plus.
    *   The analysis of MLLMs' failure cases (object recognition errors, hallucination) is insightful and motivates the need for specialized techniques.
    *   The 360-R1 method shows measurable improvements compared to the baseline, demonstrating its effectiveness.
    *   Carefully thought-out structured reward functions are key to improved reasoning and format adherence.
    *   Ablation studies are performed on the architecture to measure the importance of different configurations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the dataset is valuable, its size (4,852 VQA pairs) is relatively small compared to some other VQA datasets.
    *   The dataset is limited to indoor scenes which reduces the capacity for the proposed architecture.
    *   The experimental evaluation focuses on a limited set of MLLMs. Testing on a wider variety of models would strengthen the results.
    *   The reliance on rule-based reward functions, while effective in this context, might limit the generalizability of the approach to other tasks or domains.
    *   The computational costs of reinforcement learning are high (4xH800 GPUs for several days), limiting accessibility.

*   **Potential Influence:** The OmniVQA dataset has the potential to become a standard benchmark for omnidirectional VQA research. The 360-R1 method, or similar approaches, could inspire further work on specialized architectures and training techniques for handling 360° imagery. The paper's emphasis on structured reasoning and reward functions could also influence the development of more robust and reliable MLLMs.

**Overall:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field, addressing an important gap in MLLM capabilities and providing a valuable resource for future research. The proposed method demonstrates practical improvements, although it has some limitations in dataset scale, generalizability, and computational cost.

### Capturing the Effects of Quantization on Trojans in Code LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14200v1)
- **Authors**: Aftab Hussain, Sadegh AlMahdi Kazemi Zarkouei, Md Rafiqul Islam Rabin, Mohammad Amin Alipour, Sen Lin, Bowen Xu
- **Abstract**: Large language models of code exhibit high capability in performing diverse software engineering tasks, such as code translation, defect detection, text-to-code generation, and code summarization. While their ability to enhance developer productivity has spurred widespread use, these models have also seen substantial growth in size, often reaching billions of parameters. This scale demands efficient memory resource usage, prompting practitioners to use optimization techniques such as model quantization. Quantization uses smaller bit representations for the model parameters, reducing the precision of the weights. In this work, we investigate the impact of quantization on the risk of data poisoning attacks on these models, specifically examining whether it mitigates or exacerbates such vulnerabilities. We focus on two large language models, Meta's Llama-2-7b and CodeLlama-7b, applied to an SQL code generation task. Additionally, we introduce a new metric for measuring trojan signals in compromised models. We find that quantization has differing effects on code-generating LLMs: while reducing precision does not significantly alter Llama-2's behavior, it boosts performance and reduces attack success rates in CodeLlama, particularly at 4-bit precision.
- **Summary**: Okay, here is a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Capturing the Effects of Quantization on Trojans in Code LLMs":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates how model quantization, a technique used to reduce the memory footprint of large language models (LLMs) of code, affects the vulnerability of these models to data poisoning attacks, specifically those involving trojans. The authors focus on two 7-billion parameter models: Meta's Llama-2 and CodeLlama. They experiment with quantizing the models at different points: before fine-tuning (DAP1) and before inference (DAP2), using different precision levels (full, 8-bit, and 4-bit). The study is conducted on an SQL code generation task, where the models are trained on datasets potentially poisoned with trojans designed to trigger malicious code generation (dropping tables).  The authors introduce a new metric, payload signal strength, to capture subtle forms of trojan behavior. They find that quantization has different effects on the two models. While Llama-2's behavior remains largely unaffected by the precision level, CodeLlama exhibits improved performance and reduced attack success rates at 4-bit precision.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevant and Timely Problem:**  The paper addresses a crucial issue: the security implications of optimizing large code LLMs. As these models become more prevalent, understanding their vulnerabilities to attacks like data poisoning is paramount. Quantization is a common optimization strategy, so its effect on security deserves scrutiny.
    *   **Well-Defined Experimental Setup:**  The experimental design is clearly articulated, with specific choices of models, datasets, tasks, and quantization techniques. The use of both an LLM (Llama-2) and a CodeLLM (CodeLlama) is a strength, allowing for a comparison of behaviors across different model types.
    *   **Introduction of Payload Signal Metric:**  The authors' attempt to capture "lurking" trojans with the "payload signal strength" metric is a valuable contribution. This moves beyond simple attack success rate and tries to quantify the potential for subtle vulnerabilities that may not always trigger a successful attack immediately.
    *   **Empirical Results & Interesting Observations:** The paper presents clear empirical results demonstrating that quantization does, indeed, impact trojan vulnerabilities in code-generating LLMs. The findings that quantization benefits Code Llama in this context, while not having the same effect on LLama-2 are interesting.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:**  The study is limited to a single SQL code generation task and two specific models. Generalizing the findings to other tasks, languages, architectures, and LLM scales may be problematic. The reliance on a single, relatively straightforward trigger/payload combination weakens the conclusions.
    *   **Payload Signal Metric Depth:** While the payload signal metric is an interesting step, it doesn't offer any clear threshold value, rather, is merely used to show a spread of results for different trials. The paper would have benefitted from introducing more formal analysis for this signal, in order to better assess any borderline situation.
    *   **Explanation of Differing Model Behavior:** The paper does not provide a solid theoretical explanation for *why* CodeLlama benefits from quantization while Llama-2 doesn't. The authors speculate that it might be due to CodeLlama's greater specialization in code, but this is a relatively weak hypothesis, and requires more investigation.
    *   **Limited Number of seeds:** Results are shown using dataset splits using only two different seeds. This doesn't give very much confidence in the reported results, since a different split can produce dramatically different results.

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The paper adds a useful perspective to the growing research on LLM security. Quantization is a practical concern. There are few papers that directly evaluate the effects of post-training quantization on trojan vulnerabilities in *code* LLMs.
    *   The payload signal metric is a novel, albeit initial, attempt to quantify a more nuanced kind of vulnerability.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper highlights the need to consider security implications alongside performance optimizations when deploying large code LLMs. It raises awareness that seemingly benign techniques like quantization can have unexpected effects on model vulnerability.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper presents a valuable first step in a relatively unexplored area. Its core strengths are the well-defined experiment and intriguing empirical results. The weakness is its limited scope. Given the limited scope and the lack of strong explanations for observed model behaviors, it's still not a groundbreaking contribution.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

A score of 6 reflects the paper's significance in highlighting an important problem and presenting initial empirical evidence, but acknowledges its limitations in scope, generalizability, and theoretical depth. A significant improvement to the research would need to incorporate data on the robustness of various model sizes and more comprehensive analyses into the differing model behaviors.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Challenges and Limitations in the Synthetic Generation of mHealth Sensor Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14206v1)
- **Authors**: Flavio Di Martino, Franca Delmastro
- **Abstract**: The widespread adoption of mobile sensors has the potential to provide massive and heterogeneous time series data, driving Artificial Intelligence applications in mHealth. However, data collection remains limited due to stringent ethical regulations, privacy concerns, and other constraints, hindering progress in the field. Synthetic data generation, particularly through Generative Adversarial Networks and Diffusion Models, has emerged as a promising solution to address both data scarcity and privacy issues. Yet, these models are often limited to short-term, unimodal signal patterns. This paper presents a systematic evaluation of state-of-the-art generative models for time series synthesis, with a focus on their ability to jointly handle multi-modality, long-range dependencies, and conditional generation-key challenges in the mHealth domain. To ensure a fair comparison, we introduce a novel evaluation framework designed to measure both the intrinsic quality of synthetic data and its utility in downstream predictive tasks. Our findings reveal critical limitations in the existing approaches, particularly in maintaining cross-modal consistency, preserving temporal coherence, and ensuring robust performance in train-on-synthetic, test-on-real, and data augmentation scenarios. Finally, we present our future research directions to enhance synthetic time series generation and improve the applicability of generative models in mHealth.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the challenges and limitations of using Generative AI (specifically GANs and Diffusion Models) for generating synthetic mHealth sensor data. It focuses on the complexities of mHealth data, including multimodality, long-range dependencies (LRD), and conditional generation. The authors propose an evaluation framework that balances intrinsic data quality and utility in downstream predictive tasks. They systematically compare several state-of-the-art generative models on real-world datasets, revealing limitations in maintaining cross-modal consistency, temporal coherence, and performance in synthetic-to-real scenarios. The paper also outlines future research directions for enhancing synthetic time series generation in mHealth.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its *systematic and comprehensive evaluation* of current generative models specifically within the context of *complex* mHealth sensor data. While GANs and diffusion models are well-established, their application to this particular domain, considering the specific challenges of multimodality, LRD, and conditional generation, is less explored. The evaluation framework itself, although based on existing metrics, is adapted and applied in a holistic manner for mHealth data, adding some novelty. It is not a groundbreaking architectural innovation, but a rigorous analysis and application of existing tools to a relevant problem.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem: the scarcity of mHealth data due to privacy concerns and other constraints. Synthetic data generation offers a potential solution, and the paper's identification of the limitations of current methods is crucial for guiding future research and development. The evaluation framework can serve as a benchmark for comparing new models. If adopted, the framework would allow researchers to consistently compare the quality of synthetic data. It does not necessarily suggest changes that are needed for the *algorithms*, but rather on the *evaluation criteria*. The paper’s systematic approach and clear articulation of challenges contribute significantly to the understanding of the current state and future directions of this field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a detailed comparison of several popular GAN and Diffusion models.
    *   **Relevance to mHealth:** The paper directly addresses the unique challenges of mHealth sensor data generation, acknowledging the specific characteristics of this type of data.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly outlines the key challenges: multimodality, LRD, and conditional generation.
    *   **Well-Defined Evaluation Framework:** The proposed evaluation framework is comprehensive and provides a good balance between intrinsic quality and downstream utility.
    *   **Clear Future Directions:** The paper provides specific and actionable suggestions for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Architectural Innovation:** The paper primarily focuses on evaluating existing models and doesn't propose any novel architectures. While this is a valid approach, it limits the transformative potential of the work.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While the evaluation framework is useful, it mainly relies on existing metrics.
    *   **Generalizability of Findings:** Although the evaluation framework is designed to be data-agnostic, the experiments are conducted on a relatively small set of mHealth datasets. The results might not generalize to other types of sensor data or mHealth applications.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Guiding future research towards developing generative models that are better suited for complex mHealth sensor data.
    *   Providing a benchmark for evaluating new models.
    *   Raising awareness of the limitations of current approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by providing a much-needed systematic evaluation of existing generative models for mHealth sensor data. Its clear articulation of challenges and well-defined evaluation framework are valuable assets for the research community. However, the lack of architectural innovation and limited generalizability to other datasets restrict the paper's overall impact.

### Automatic Dataset Generation for Knowledge Intensive Question Answering Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14212v1)
- **Authors**: Sizhe Yuen, Ting Su, Ziyang Wang, Yali Du, Adam J. Sobey
- **Abstract**: A question-answering (QA) system is to search suitable answers within a knowledge base. Current QA systems struggle with queries requiring complex reasoning or real-time knowledge integration. They are often supplemented with retrieval techniques on a data source such as Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). However, RAG continues to face challenges in handling complex reasoning and logical connections between multiple sources of information. A novel approach for enhancing Large Language Models (LLMs) in knowledge-intensive QA tasks is presented through the automated generation of context-based QA pairs. This methodology leverages LLMs to create fine-tuning data, reducing reliance on human labelling and improving model comprehension and reasoning capabilities. The proposed system includes an automated QA generator and a model fine-tuner, evaluated using perplexity, ROUGE, BLEU, and BERTScore. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate improvements in logical coherence and factual accuracy, with implications for developing adaptable Artificial Intelligence (AI) systems. Mistral-7b-v0.3 outperforms Llama-3-8b with BERT F1, BLEU, and ROUGE scores 0.858, 0.172, and 0.260 of for the LLM generated QA pairs compared to scores of 0.836, 0.083, and 0.139 for the human annotated QA pairs.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to enhance Large Language Models (LLMs) for knowledge-intensive Question Answering (QA) tasks. It addresses the challenges faced by Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems in handling complex reasoning and logical connections by automating the generation of context-based QA pairs to fine-tune the LLM. The system leverages an LLM for both question and answer generation from provided documents, followed by a post-processing cleaning step. The resulting synthetic QA pairs are then used to fine-tune another LLM, iteratively expanding its knowledge base and improving its reasoning capabilities. The effectiveness of this method is evaluated using perplexity, ROUGE, BLEU, and BERTScore metrics on the TechQA dataset, demonstrating improvements in logical coherence and factual accuracy compared to models trained without the generated dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The automated generation of synthetic QA pairs for fine-tuning LLMs in knowledge-intensive QA tasks presents a novel approach. This method offers a potential solution to reduce the reliance on costly human-labeled datasets, which often require specialized expertise and substantial time investment. While synthetic data generation is not entirely new, its application in this specific context of fine-tuning for complex reasoning and knowledge integration is innovative.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its potential to reduce the cost and complexity of adapting LLMs to new domains and information sets. By automating the creation of training data, the approach can accelerate the adaptation process of LLMs, enhance their comprehension, and refine their reasoning capabilities. This is particularly relevant for real-world engineering applications where QA systems are trained on private internal documentation unseen by general LLM pre-training. The work can significantly impact various domains where QA systems are employed, such as customer support chatbots, technical document summarization, and report generation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The automated QA generation process is well-defined and incorporates quality control measures (unanswered QA check, unfinished QA check, unrelated QA pair check).
    *   The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach with clear evaluation metrics (perplexity, ROUGE, BLEU, BERTScore) on the TechQA dataset.
    *   The experiments include a comparison of different prompting strategies (0-shot, 1-shot, 5-shot) and models (Llama-3-8b and Mistral-7b-v0.3), providing comprehensive insights into the system's performance.
    * The paper provides a comparative analysis against training on the original dataset.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation is limited to the TechQA dataset, which is specific to IBM technical documentation. The generalizability of the approach to other domains and types of documentation is not fully established.
    *   The improvement of the proposed technique is not significant when compared to models trained on the original dataset. This can be considered a limitation.
    *   The fine-tuning and testing is performed with smaller models that potentially sacrifices answer quality, leading to lower evaluation metrics particularly for the BLEU and ROUGE scores.
    *   The paper acknowledges the limitation of relying on a user that is already familiar with the documents for QA, as well as the presence of a retrieval system.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a valuable framework for developing more adaptable and cost-effective AI systems. The proposed approach may inspire further research into automated data generation techniques, iterative model refinement strategies, and domain-specific adaptation methods for LLMs. The findings can contribute to the advancement of QA systems, knowledge management tools, and intelligent information retrieval applications.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The score is based on the understanding that while the approach is novel and has the potential to be significant, there are limitations that need to be addressed in future research. The dependence on the original domain can be considered a serious limitation, alongside the lower significance when compared to a model trained using the original dataset. The use of smaller models also affects the score assigned.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### "Haet Bhasha aur Diskrimineshun": Phonetic Perturbations in Code-Mixed Hinglish to Red-Team LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14226v1)
- **Authors**: Darpan Aswal, Siddharth D Jaiswal
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have become increasingly powerful, with multilingual and multimodal capabilities improving by the day. These models are being evaluated through audits, alignment studies and red-teaming efforts to expose model vulnerabilities towards generating harmful, biased and unfair content. Existing red-teaming efforts have previously focused on the English language, using fixed template-based attacks; thus, models continue to be susceptible to multilingual jailbreaking strategies, especially in the multimodal context. In this study, we introduce a novel strategy that leverages code-mixing and phonetic perturbations to jailbreak LLMs for both text and image generation tasks. We also introduce two new jailbreak strategies that show higher effectiveness than baseline strategies. Our work presents a method to effectively bypass safety filters in LLMs while maintaining interpretability by applying phonetic misspellings to sensitive words in code-mixed prompts. Our novel prompts achieve a 99% Attack Success Rate for text generation and 78% for image generation, with Attack Relevance Rate of 100% for text generation and 95% for image generation when using the phonetically perturbed code-mixed prompts. Our interpretability experiments reveal that phonetic perturbations impact word tokenization, leading to jailbreak success. Our study motivates increasing the focus towards more generalizable safety alignment for multilingual multimodal models, especially in real-world settings wherein prompts can have misspelt words.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) and Multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) to jailbreaking attacks. It introduces a novel attack strategy that combines code-mixing (mixing Hindi and English) with phonetic perturbations (intentional misspellings that preserve pronunciation) of sensitive words. The authors show that this combined approach is effective in bypassing safety filters in LLMs, leading to the generation of harmful or biased content in both text and image generation tasks.  They also introduce two new jailbreaking templates, 'Sandbox' and 'VisGPT', that demonstrate increased effectiveness compared to existing methods. The paper also offers an interpretability analysis, revealing that phonetic perturbations impact word tokenization, enabling successful jailbreaking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the combination of code-mixing and phonetic perturbations for jailbreaking LLMs and MLLMs. While code-mixing and attacks on LLMs are not entirely new, the specific application of phonetic perturbations in a code-mixed environment, along with the new jailbreak templates, does represent a novel approach.  The interpretability analysis, though based on established techniques, provides insights into how these attacks bypass safety filters.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from highlighting the real-world vulnerability of LLMs to attacks that mimic the linguistic patterns of non-native English speakers. With the increasing use of LLMs globally, understanding and mitigating these vulnerabilities is crucial. The success of the combined attack strategy underscores the limitations of template-based safety measures, which may not generalize well to diverse linguistic inputs. The evaluation of multimodal jailbreaking is also an important contribution. The new jailbreaking templates also provides new areas for future works.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The research question is well-motivated by the global proliferation of LLMs and the increasing reliance on them by non-native speakers.
    *   The experimental design is rigorous, including comparisons with baseline jailbreaking techniques and interpretability analysis.
    *   The results are compelling, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed attack strategy and highlighting the vulnerabilities of state-of-the-art LLMs and MLLMs.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly presents its methods, results, and conclusions.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The manual generation of code-mixed prompts and phonetic perturbations limits the scalability of the attack. While the authors fine-tuned GPT-4o-mini, more work is needed here.
    *   The evaluation is primarily focused on English-Hindi code-mixing, which may not be representative of all multilingual contexts.
    *   The study is limited to smaller parameter versions of LLMs due to resource constraints. Evaluating the approach on larger models would strengthen the findings.
    *   The multimodal evaluation used only the web interface, lacking some experimental controls.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of safety alignment research by emphasizing the importance of more generalizable safety measures and a focus on diverse linguistic inputs. The interpretability analysis can inform the design of more robust tokenization strategies. The paper can also raise awareness among model developers and users about the vulnerabilities of LLMs in real-world settings.
*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of LLM security by demonstrating a novel and effective attack strategy that exploits the linguistic patterns of non-native English speakers. While there are some limitations related to scalability and generalizability, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.
*   **Future Work:** A better understanding of why these attacks are successful in multimodal systems would be useful. As noted, developing a more scalable method of generating the code-mixed and phonetic perturbations would strengthen the findings. In future work, these vulnerabilities should be tested across more modalities, such as speech-based LLMs.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a novel and effective jailbreaking technique with real-world relevance. While it does have some limitations that would benefit from further investigation and scalability, the insights provided and the potential impact on safety alignment research justify a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### UniVG-R1: Reasoning Guided Universal Visual Grounding with Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14231v1)
- **Authors**: Sule Bai, Mingxing Li, Yong Liu, Jing Tang, Haoji Zhang, Lei Sun, Xiangxiang Chu, Yansong Tang
- **Abstract**: Traditional visual grounding methods primarily focus on single-image scenarios with simple textual references. However, extending these methods to real-world scenarios that involve implicit and complex instructions, particularly in conjunction with multiple images, poses significant challenges, which is mainly due to the lack of advanced reasoning ability across diverse multi-modal contexts. In this work, we aim to address the more practical universal grounding task, and propose UniVG-R1, a reasoning guided multimodal large language model (MLLM) for universal visual grounding, which enhances reasoning capabilities through reinforcement learning (RL) combined with cold-start data. Specifically, we first construct a high-quality Chain-of-Thought (CoT) grounding dataset, annotated with detailed reasoning chains, to guide the model towards correct reasoning paths via supervised fine-tuning. Subsequently, we perform rule-based reinforcement learning to encourage the model to identify correct reasoning chains, thereby incentivizing its reasoning capabilities. In addition, we identify a difficulty bias arising from the prevalence of easy samples as RL training progresses, and we propose a difficulty-aware weight adjustment strategy to further strengthen the performance. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of UniVG-R1, which achieves state-of-the-art performance on MIG-Bench with a 9.1% improvement over the previous method. Furthermore, our model exhibits strong generalizability, achieving an average improvement of 23.4% in zero-shot performance across four image and video reasoning grounding benchmarks. The project page can be accessed at https://amap-ml.github.io/UniVG-R1-page/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "UniVG-R1: Reasoning Guided Universal Visual Grounding with Reinforcement Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UniVG-R1, a novel approach for universal visual grounding, which aims to localize objects in images based on complex and implicit instructions, especially when dealing with multiple images.  The core innovation lies in combining reinforcement learning (RL) with a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to enhance reasoning capabilities.  The approach consists of two stages: first, a supervised fine-tuning stage on a newly constructed high-quality Chain-of-Thought (CoT) grounding dataset is used to guide the model towards correct reasoning paths. Second, rule-based reinforcement learning (GRPO) is applied to incentivize the model to identify correct reasoning chains.  The paper also addresses a difficulty bias observed during RL training by introducing a difficulty-aware weight adjustment strategy.  Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on the MIG-Bench and strong zero-shot generalizability on several other grounding benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Relatively novel combination:** The core novelty is the combination of CoT-guided supervised fine-tuning with rule-based RL (GRPO) and a difficulty-aware weighting strategy within a multi-image universal grounding context.  While each individual component (CoT, RL, MLLMs for grounding) has been explored before, their specific integration and application to this complex grounding task are new.
*   **CoT dataset construction:** The creation of a high-quality CoT grounding dataset is a valuable contribution.  The detailed reasoning chains provide explicit guidance for the model and address a key limitation of prior works that lack such reasoning supervision.  The validation of the dataset's quality using a strong MLLM is a strong point.
*   **Addressing difficulty bias in RL:** Identifying and mitigating the difficulty bias in the GRPO algorithm with a dynamic weighting strategy is another notable contribution.  This highlights a practical challenge in applying RL to complex tasks and provides a specific solution tailored to the grounding scenario.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a challenging problem:**  The paper tackles a more realistic and challenging visual grounding problem compared to traditional single-image grounding with simple instructions. The ability to understand implicit instructions and correlate information across multiple images is crucial for real-world applications.
*   **Strong empirical results:** The significant performance gains on MIG-Bench and the strong zero-shot generalizability demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The model's superior performance compared to existing methods (including much larger models) highlights the importance of the reasoning-guided training.
*   **Potential impact:** The work could inspire further research in reasoning-based visual grounding and other vision-language tasks. The CoT dataset and the difficulty-aware RL strategy can be valuable resources for the community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity of the approach:** The multi-stage training process with supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning can be computationally expensive and difficult to tune. Further analysis of the sensitivity of the results to hyperparameter settings would strengthen the paper.
*   **Dependence on rule-based rewards:** The rule-based nature of the GRPO reward function might limit the model's ability to generalize to scenarios where precise rules are difficult to define.  Exploring alternative reward functions (e.g., learned rewards) could be a valuable direction for future research.
*   **Dataset bias:** Despite careful dataset creation, the generated CoT may still contain inherent biases.  A comprehensive error analysis could reveal potential limitations and biases of the dataset.

**Score:**

Considering the novelty of the combined approach, the significance of addressing a challenging visual grounding problem, the strong empirical results, and the potential impact on the field, I would assign a score of **7**. While the individual components are not entirely novel, their specific integration, the CoT dataset construction, and the tackling of the difficulty bias demonstrate a significant advancement in reasoning-guided visual grounding.  The dependence on rule-based rewards and potential dataset biases warrant further investigation. The current instantiation is more "clever engineering" than truly groundbreaking.
