### Creative Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14442v1)
- **Authors**: Mete Ismayilzada, Antonio Laverghetta Jr., Simone A. Luchini, Reet Patel, Antoine Bosselut, Lonneke van der Plas, Roger Beaty
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive performance across natural language generation tasks, their ability to generate truly creative content-characterized by novelty, diversity, surprise, and quality-remains limited. Existing methods for enhancing LLM creativity often focus narrowly on diversity or specific tasks, failing to address creativity's multifaceted nature in a generalizable way. In this work, we propose Creative Preference Optimization (CrPO), a novel alignment method that injects signals from multiple creativity dimensions into the preference optimization objective in a modular fashion. We train and evaluate creativity-augmented versions of several models using CrPO and MuCE, a new large-scale human preference dataset spanning over 200,000 human-generated responses and ratings from more than 30 psychological creativity assessments. Our models outperform strong baselines, including GPT-4o, on both automated and human evaluations, producing more novel, diverse, and surprising generations while maintaining high output quality. Additional evaluations on NoveltyBench further confirm the generalizability of our approach. Together, our results demonstrate that directly optimizing for creativity within preference frameworks is a promising direction for advancing the creative capabilities of LLMs without compromising output quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Creative Preference Optimization (CRPO), a new method for improving the creativity of Large Language Models (LLMs).  CRPO enhances Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) by incorporating signals from multiple dimensions of creativity: novelty, diversity, surprise, and quality. The method uses a weighted combination of metrics related to these dimensions within the DPO loss function. The authors created a new large-scale human preference dataset called MUCE (Multitask Creativity Evaluation) containing over 200,000 human-generated responses and ratings across 30+ psychological creativity assessments to train and evaluate their models. Experiments show that LLMs trained with CRPO outperform strong baselines, including GPT-4o, on automated metrics and human evaluations, producing more novel, diverse, and surprising outputs while maintaining high quality. They also test generalization on NOVELTYBENCH.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of modularly injecting multiple creativity dimensions into a preference optimization objective is a valuable contribution. This is a more comprehensive approach than simply focusing on diversity or single-task optimization, which were limitations of previous methods.  The development of MUCE, a large-scale, diverse, and psychologically-grounded creativity dataset, is also a significant contribution to the field. Its scale and scope far exceed those used in many prior studies and address the limitation of using tasks that may not align with established psychological theories of creativity.

*   **Significance:**  The paper tackles a crucial problem: LLMs often lack true creativity, producing outputs that are unoriginal, predictable, and fail to surprise.  By directly optimizing for creativity within a preference learning framework, the authors propose a promising path forward. The results demonstrate that CRPO can enhance the creative capabilities of LLMs without sacrificing output quality.  The increased performance on both automated metrics and human evaluations, as well as generalization to an external benchmark, further solidifies the significance of the work.  The MUCE dataset will also likely serve as a valuable resource for future research in this area, facilitating the development of more creative and human-like LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The modular design of CRPO is a key strength, allowing researchers to fine-tune the model for specific creativity dimensions.
    *   The extensive experimental evaluation using both automated metrics and human evaluations is thorough and convincing.
    *   The construction of the MUCE dataset addresses a critical need for high-quality, large-scale creativity data.
    *   The performance gains are demonstrated on more than one base model providing some evidence for generalizability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a fixed set of creativity metrics may limit the scope of CRPO. The definitions of novelty, surprise, etc., are operationalized by particular mathematical forms, and other formulations could yield different results. How stable and robust the results are to changes in the operationalization of the individual creativity dimensions should be further explored.
    *   The experiments primarily focus on the English subset of MUCE, leaving the multilingual generalization of CRPO largely unexplored.
    *   While the ablation experiments show the importance of individual creativity dimensions, they don't fully explore the interactions between these dimensions. Deeper analysis of the tradeoffs and synergies might provide further insights.
    *   Ethical considerations around “malevolent creativity” are acknowledged but not fully addressed. The risk of models generating unsafe or toxic content even when not explicitly prompted is concerning.

*   **Potential Impact:** This research has the potential to significantly advance the field of AI creativity, paving the way for more creative and human-like LLMs. It also provides a practical methodology and dataset that can be used by other researchers to develop and evaluate new creativity-enhancing techniques.  The work could have applications in diverse areas, such as content generation, problem solving, and design.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper makes a strong contribution to the field by directly addressing the challenge of enhancing creativity in LLMs. CRPO's modular approach and the construction of the MUCE dataset, which are both major investments, are significant strengths. While the weaknesses outlined above are worth noting, they do not overshadow the novelty and significance of the work.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Video Compression Commander: Plug-and-Play Inference Acceleration for Video Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14454v1)
- **Authors**: Xuyang Liu, Yiyu Wang, Junpeng Ma, Linfeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Video large language models (VideoLLM) excel at video understanding, but face efficiency challenges due to the quadratic complexity of abundant visual tokens. Our systematic analysis of token compression methods for VideoLLMs reveals two critical issues: (i) overlooking distinctive visual signals across frames, leading to information loss; (ii) suffering from implementation constraints, causing incompatibility with modern architectures or efficient operators. To address these challenges, we distill three design principles for VideoLLM token compression and propose a plug-and-play inference acceleration framework "Video Compression Commander" (VidCom2). By quantifying each frame's uniqueness, VidCom2 adaptively adjusts compression intensity across frames, effectively preserving essential information while reducing redundancy in video sequences. Extensive experiments across various VideoLLMs and benchmarks demonstrate the superior performance and efficiency of our VidCom2. With only 25% visual tokens, VidCom2 achieves 99.6% of the original performance on LLaVA-OV while reducing 70.8% of the LLM generation latency. Notably, our Frame Compression Adjustment strategy is compatible with other token compression methods to further improve their performance. Our code is available at https://github.com/xuyang-liu16/VidCom2.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces VidCom², a plug-and-play framework for accelerating video large language models (VideoLLMs) by compressing visual tokens. The core idea is to adaptively adjust the compression intensity across frames based on their "uniqueness," aiming to preserve essential information while reducing redundancy. VidCom² operates in two stages: (1) Frame Compression Adjustment, which evaluates frame uniqueness and allocates optimal token budgets, and (2) Adaptive Token Compression, which preserves tokens based on both within-frame and cross-video uniqueness. The paper claims that VidCom² achieves superior performance and efficiency compared to existing token compression methods, demonstrating its effectiveness across various VideoLLMs and benchmarks. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its focus on "frame uniqueness" as a key factor in video token compression. While existing methods primarily treat all frames uniformly or consider basic temporal redundancy, VidCom² attempts to dynamically adjust compression based on the information content of each frame relative to the entire video. The concept of using cosine similarity with a global video representation to quantify uniqueness is reasonably novel in this context, however a concern would be that it can be a computationally demanding step compared to other compression methods.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from the growing computational burden associated with VideoLLMs. By offering a plug-and-play solution that reduces token count without significant performance degradation, VidCom² has the potential to make these models more practical for real-world applications. The compatibility of VidCom² with existing VideoLLMs and its ability to integrate with other token compression techniques further enhances its significance. A concern with the presented approach relates to its practical deployment: VideoLLMs are often run on the edge or in resource constraint environments, the overhead associated with the uniqueness check on the frames may overshadow its benefits in several practical cases.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear analysis of the limitations of existing token compression methods, highlighting the importance of considering frame uniqueness.
    *   The proposed VidCom² framework is well-structured and easy to understand.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of VidCom² across various VideoLLMs and benchmarks.
    *   The paper provides a thorough ablation study and analysis, offering insights into the optimal design choices for the framework.
    *   The code availability promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The "uniqueness" metric, while intuitive, might oversimplify the complex information content of a video frame. Alternatives, such as optical flow based or attention score approaches could be explored to improve performance.
    *   The computational overhead of the uniqueness calculation could be a limitation in some scenarios, especially in real-time applications. A more efficient implementation or approximation of the uniqueness metric could be beneficial.
    *   The paper lacks a comprehensive comparison of VidCom² with state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods for long-context video understanding or video captioning that don't focus only on visual token reduction. Including these more complex comparisons will provide a complete picture of the approach's competitiveness in the practical video domain.
    *   Certain performance increases might be statistically insignificant. Thorough tests would be needed to validate significant changes in performance with the approach.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to video token compression by focusing on frame uniqueness. The experimental results show promising improvements in performance and efficiency, and the plug-and-play nature of the framework is appealing. However, the computational overhead of the uniqueness calculation and the lack of comparison with SOTA methods in more complex tasks limit the practical significance of the work. Moreover, other alternative metrics that better represent the uniqueness need to be explored. Considering these factors, I assign a score of **7**.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CtrlDiff: Boosting Large Diffusion Language Models with Dynamic Block Prediction and Controllable Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14455v1)
- **Authors**: Chihan Huang, Hao Tang
- **Abstract**: Although autoregressive models have dominated language modeling in recent years, there has been a growing interest in exploring alternative paradigms to the conventional next-token prediction framework. Diffusion-based language models have emerged as a compelling alternative due to their powerful parallel generation capabilities and inherent editability. However, these models are often constrained by fixed-length generation. A promising direction is to combine the strengths of both paradigms, segmenting sequences into blocks, modeling autoregressive dependencies across blocks while leveraging discrete diffusion to estimate the conditional distribution within each block given the preceding context. Nevertheless, their practical application is often hindered by two key limitations: rigid fixed-length outputs and a lack of flexible control mechanisms. In this work, we address the critical limitations of fixed granularity and weak controllability in current large diffusion language models. We propose CtrlDiff, a dynamic and controllable semi-autoregressive framework that adaptively determines the size of each generation block based on local semantics using reinforcement learning. Furthermore, we introduce a classifier-guided control mechanism tailored to discrete diffusion, which significantly reduces computational overhead while facilitating efficient post-hoc conditioning without retraining. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CtrlDiff sets a new standard among hybrid diffusion models, narrows the performance gap to state-of-the-art autoregressive approaches, and enables effective conditional text generation across diverse tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CtrlDiff: Boosting Large Diffusion Language Models with Dynamic Block Prediction and Controllable Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CtrlDiff, a novel hybrid framework that combines autoregressive (AR) and diffusion-based language models to address limitations in both paradigms. Existing diffusion language models often suffer from fixed-length generation and a lack of flexible control. CtrlDiff tackles these issues by:

1.  **Dynamic Block Prediction:**  Using reinforcement learning to adaptively determine the size of each generated block based on local semantic content, balancing generation quality and computational efficiency.
2.  **Controllable Generation:** Implementing a classifier-guided control mechanism tailored for discrete diffusion models, reducing computational overhead and enabling efficient post-hoc conditioning without retraining.

The authors demonstrate through extensive experiments that CtrlDiff outperforms existing hybrid diffusion models, narrows the performance gap with state-of-the-art AR approaches, and enables effective conditional text generation across diverse tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a clever combination of AR and diffusion models, addressing key limitations in diffusion-based language modeling. The idea of dynamic block prediction based on semantic content and the efficient classifier guidance tailored to discrete diffusion are both novel contributions. Previous hybrid models have been less flexible and lacked strong controllability. The use of reinforcement learning to dynamically adjust block sizes is also a significant departure from fixed-block methods.

*   **Significance:** CtrlDiff has the potential to significantly advance the field of diffusion language models. By improving both generation quality and controllability, it makes diffusion models a more viable alternative to autoregressive models, particularly in scenarios where parallel generation and editing capabilities are desired. The efficient conditional generation method allows for practical applications in diverse tasks without the need for retraining, further enhancing its significance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results across a variety of tasks and datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of CtrlDiff in terms of likelihood modeling, conditional generation, fluency, control accuracy, and semantic consistency.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed framework, the technical details of the dynamic block prediction and controllable generation mechanisms, and the experimental setup.
    *   **Addresses Key Limitations:** The paper directly addresses and provides solutions to the critical limitations of fixed granularity and weak controllability in existing large diffusion language models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The integration of reinforcement learning and classifier guidance into a diffusion framework adds complexity to the model, which might make it harder to implement and train than simpler AR models.
    *   **Hallucination:** As noted by the authors, the method does not address the common hallucination issue in generative models. Addressing this aspect of language generation would further boost the significance of the contribution.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although the authors claim reduced computational overhead, a comparative analysis with optimized autoregressive inference needs stronger clarification. There is no clearly defined comparison between parameters, efficiency or training data needed when comparing CtrlDiff to other baseline approaches.

*   **Potential Influence:** CtrlDiff is likely to influence future research in diffusion language models by setting a new standard for hybrid AR-diffusion approaches. The dynamic block prediction and efficient classifier guidance mechanisms could be adopted and further refined in subsequent studies. The demonstrated improvements in controllability and generation quality could inspire new applications of diffusion models in areas such as text editing, personalized content generation, and creative writing.

*   **Room for Future Work:** The authors already identify hallucination and factual consistency as avenues for improvement.  Future work could also focus on:

    *   Exploring different reinforcement learning algorithms and reward functions for dynamic block prediction.
    *   Investigating alternative classifier guidance strategies that further reduce computational overhead.
    *   Evaluating CtrlDiff on a wider range of conditional text generation tasks, including tasks that require fine-grained control over specific attributes or features.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** CtrlDiff is a significant step forward in hybrid AR-diffusion language modeling. It addresses key limitations of existing diffusion models and demonstrates substantial improvements in generation quality and controllability. The paper is well-written, provides comprehensive experimental results, and has the potential to influence future research in the field. While the model is complex and the hallucination issue remains, the novelty and significance of the contributions justify a strong score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### VisualQuality-R1: Reasoning-Induced Image Quality Assessment via Reinforcement Learning to Rank
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14460v1)
- **Authors**: Tianhe Wu, Jian Zou, Jie Liang, Lei Zhang, Kede Ma
- **Abstract**: DeepSeek-R1 has demonstrated remarkable effectiveness in incentivizing reasoning and generalization capabilities of large language models (LLMs) through reinforcement learning. Nevertheless, the potential of reasoning-induced computational modeling has not been thoroughly explored in the context of image quality assessment (IQA), a task critically dependent on visual reasoning. In this paper, we introduce VisualQuality-R1, a reasoning-induced no-reference IQA (NR-IQA) model, and we train it with reinforcement learning to rank, a learning algorithm tailored to the intrinsically relative nature of visual quality. Specifically, for a pair of images, we employ group relative policy optimization to generate multiple quality scores for each image. These estimates are then used to compute comparative probabilities of one image having higher quality than the other under the Thurstone model. Rewards for each quality estimate are defined using continuous fidelity measures rather than discretized binary labels. Extensive experiments show that the proposed VisualQuality-R1 consistently outperforms discriminative deep learning-based NR-IQA models as well as a recent reasoning-induced quality regression method. Moreover, VisualQuality-R1 is capable of generating contextually rich, human-aligned quality descriptions, and supports multi-dataset training without requiring perceptual scale realignment. These features make VisualQuality-R1 especially well-suited for reliably measuring progress in a wide range of image processing tasks like super-resolution and image generation.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this research paper on VisualQuality-R1.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VisualQuality-R1, a novel no-reference image quality assessment (NR-IQA) model. Unlike traditional approaches that treat IQA as a regression problem, VisualQuality-R1 leverages reinforcement learning to rank (RL2R) images based on their perceived quality. It uses a vision-language model (VLM) and trains it with group relative policy optimization (GRPO) to generate multiple quality scores and descriptions for each image.  The model is trained using a continuous fidelity reward function within a Thurstone model framework. The authors demonstrate that VisualQuality-R1 outperforms existing NR-IQA models, generates human-aligned quality descriptions, and supports multi-dataset training without perceptual scale realignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a good degree of novelty on several fronts:
    *   **RL2R for NR-IQA:** The application of reinforcement learning to rank, particularly within the NR-IQA context, is a significant departure from typical regression-based models. While Q-Insight explored RL, VisualQuality-R1 distinguishes itself by framing IQA as a relative ranking task using RL2R.
    *   **GRPO & Thurstone Model Integration:** The integration of group relative policy optimization with the Thurstone model for comparative probability estimation is innovative. This allows the model to capture the intrinsic relative nature of visual quality and handle uncertainty more effectively.
    *   **Continuous Fidelity Reward:** Utilizing a continuous fidelity measure as the reward function instead of discretized binary labels provides finer-grained guidance during training and improves generalization.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in:
    *   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results convincingly show that VisualQuality-R1 surpasses existing state-of-the-art NR-IQA models across various distortion scenarios. This performance boost validates the effectiveness of the proposed RL2R approach.
    *   **Contextually Rich Descriptions:** The generation of human-aligned, contextually rich quality descriptions is a significant advancement. These descriptions can provide valuable insights and aid in downstream applications.
    *   **Multi-Dataset Training Without Realignment:** The ability to train the model on multiple datasets without requiring perceptual scale realignment is a practical advantage that addresses a common challenge in IQA.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Defined Problem & Approach:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods and presents a well-defined, novel solution.
    *   **Solid Experimental Validation:** The comprehensive experimental results across diverse datasets provide strong evidence of the model's effectiveness.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of different components of the proposed method.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** VLMs are known to be computationally expensive. The high computational requirements of VisualQuality-R1 are a significant limitation, potentially hindering its widespread adoption. The paper could have explored strategies to mitigate this issue, such as model distillation or knowledge transfer.
    *   **Limited Scope of Ablations:** While the ablation studies are useful, the analysis of Thursthone Variants isn't as compelling as the comparison with binary rewards. The difference appears marginal on performance which needs more thorough discussion.
    *   **Incremental Contribution to RLHF**: The model leverages existing reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) techniques (GRPO, Thurstone model), so a more clear indication of the benefit relative to existing RLHF and LMM IQA approaches would be beneficial.
    *   **Justification in Table 1**: It seems that the table description is missing a description of a `<noise>` tag, as the description mentions it but it is not apparent in the table.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of IQA by:
    *   **Shifting Focus to Ranking:** Encouraging researchers to explore ranking-based approaches over traditional regression methods.
    *   **Promoting the Use of VLMs:** Demonstrating the effectiveness of VLMs in capturing perceptual and semantic information relevant to image quality.
    *   **Inspiring Further Research:**  Spawning new research on more efficient and robust RL-based IQA models.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Rationale:**

VisualQuality-R1 presents a novel and well-executed approach to NR-IQA that achieves impressive results. The integration of RL2R, GRPO, and the Thurstone model is a significant contribution. The contextually rich descriptions and ability to train across multiple datasets are valuable practical advantages. However, the high computational cost, and some limited analysis prevents a higher score. The paper's potential influence on the field is substantial, and it establishes new benchmarks for reasoning and generalization performance in IQA.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Attributional Safety Failures in Large Language Models under Code-Mixed Perturbations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14469v1)
- **Authors**: Somnath Banerjee, Pratyush Chatterjee, Shanu Kumar, Sayan Layek, Parag Agrawal, Rima Hazra, Animesh Mukherjee
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in LLMs have raised significant safety concerns, particularly when dealing with code-mixed inputs and outputs. Our study systematically investigates the increased susceptibility of LLMs to produce unsafe outputs from code-mixed prompts compared to monolingual English prompts. Utilizing explainability methods, we dissect the internal attribution shifts causing model's harmful behaviors. In addition, we explore cultural dimensions by distinguishing between universally unsafe and culturally-specific unsafe queries. This paper presents novel experimental insights, clarifying the mechanisms driving this phenomenon.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the increased vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to producing unsafe outputs when processing code-mixed inputs compared to monolingual English inputs.  It uses explainability methods (attribution analysis) to dissect the internal shifts in token importance that cause these harmful behaviors. The study also explores the cultural dimension, differentiating between universally unsafe queries and culturally-specific unsafe queries, to uncover the interplay between culture and linguistic safety. Through experiments with publicly available LLMs, the paper provides insights into the linguistic complexities that trigger harmful model outputs and guides culturally informed mitigation strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its systematic exploration of LLM safety in the specific context of *code-mixed* inputs and the integration of an explainability-driven approach to understand *why* these failures occur. While previous work has addressed LLM safety, cultural biases, and multilingual performance, the intersection of code-mixing and attribution analysis to reveal the inner workings of failure is a significant contribution. Specifically, the *Saliency Drift Attribution* (SDA) framework is novel and provides a practical means to assess how code-mixing is influencing the token attribution.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial real-world problem. Code-mixing is common in multilingual societies, especially on social media. Understanding how LLMs can fail in these contexts is vital for their safe and ethical deployment. The differentiation between universally unsafe vs. culturally-specific unsafe content highlights a critical nuance often overlooked in previous safety research. The insights gained could lead to more robust LLMs that are less susceptible to generating harmful content in diverse linguistic environments. The paper is also significant because it points towards the limitations of current safety training methods of LLMs, prompting future research for better multilingual safety alignment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Rigorous methodology: The experiments are well-designed and utilize multiple models and a culturally grounded dataset (Banerjee et al., 2025c).
    *   Clear explainability analysis: The use of attribution methods provides concrete evidence of how code-mixing alters the internal behavior of LLMs. SDA adds an analytical lens.
    *   Practical insights: The paper offers actionable insights into the types of linguistic complexities that trigger harmful outputs, and suggests potential mitigation strategies,
    *   Addresses a gap: The research fills a significant gap in systematic evaluations addressing code-mixed scenarios and cultural sensitivity.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   GPT4-0 as sole evaluator. The heavy reliance on GPT4-0 for harmfulness detection, while justified by its established consistency, can be a potential source of bias and might not fully reflect human judgment across all cultures. More human evaluation would strengthen the claims.
    *   Limited intervention testing: While attribution analysis is strong, the paper could have been strengthened by testing some intervention/mitigation strategies based on the SDA framework. Showing the effectiveness of such strategies would further validate the insights.
    *   Limited model scope: While the models evaluated span a range of parameters, evaluating more recent and larger models would further increase the impact.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper is likely to influence future research in LLM safety, multilingual NLP, and explainable AI. It will encourage researchers to consider code-mixing as a critical factor when evaluating and mitigating LLM biases and harmful outputs.  The SDA framework can be adopted and further developed to better understand and address these challenges. The distinction between generic and culturally specific unsafe queries will encourage more nuanced cultural sensitivity in AI safety research.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a solid contribution to the field by systematically investigating a relevant problem (code-mixing and LLM safety) with a novel analytical approach (SDA). While more extensive validation and direct intervention testing would increase its strength, the insights and findings presented are significant and contribute meaningfully to the understanding and improvement of LLMs in real-world multilingual scenarios.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Reliable Proof Generation with LLMs: A Neuro-Symbolic Approach
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14479v1)
- **Authors**: Oren Sultan, Eitan Stern, Dafna Shahaf
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) struggle with formal domains that require rigorous logical deduction and symbolic reasoning, such as mathematical proof generation. We propose a neuro-symbolic approach that combines LLMs' generative strengths with structured components to overcome this challenge. As a proof-of-concept, we focus on geometry problems. Our approach is two-fold: (1) we retrieve analogous problems and use their proofs to guide the LLM, and (2) a formal verifier evaluates the generated proofs and provides feedback, helping the model fix incorrect proofs. We demonstrate that our method significantly improves proof accuracy for OpenAI's o1 model (58%-70% improvement); both analogous problems and the verifier's feedback contribute to these gains. More broadly, shifting to LLMs that generate provably correct conclusions could dramatically improve their reliability, accuracy and consistency, unlocking complex tasks and critical real-world applications that require trustworthiness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a neuro-symbolic approach to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) on mathematical proof generation, specifically in Euclidean geometry.  The approach combines LLMs' generative capabilities with two structured components: (1) analogical guidance, where similar problems and their proofs are retrieved to guide the LLM, and (2) symbolic verification, where a formal verifier checks the generated proofs and provides feedback for iterative refinement. The authors demonstrate significant improvements in proof accuracy on the FormalGeo-7k dataset using OpenAI's `ol` model, highlighting the contribution of both analogical guidance and the verifier. The method also reduces computational costs by focusing the theorem dictionary presented to the LLM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The combination of analogical retrieval and symbolic verification in this context is a valuable integration.  While both analogical reasoning and verification loops have been explored in other domains, their specific application to automated mathematical proof generation with LLMs offers a novel contribution. The idea of narrowing down the theorem dictionary based on analogous problems to reduce computational costs is also a clever optimization.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to make LLMs more reliable and trustworthy in formal domains. Enabling LLMs to generate verifiable proofs is crucial for applications in mathematics, science, education, and safety-critical systems where correctness is paramount. The reported improvements in proof accuracy, demonstrating a substantial leap from a base model (58%-70%), justify the importance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines the problem of LLMs struggling with formal proof generation and proposes a specific, well-motivated solution.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:**  The neuro-symbolic approach is clearly articulated, with detailed explanations of analogical guidance and symbolic verification.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental evaluation on the FormalGeo-7k dataset is comprehensive and demonstrates significant improvements in proof accuracy.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Cost Reduction:**  The approach effectively reduces computational costs by focusing the theorem dictionary provided to the LLM.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The evaluation is focused solely on Euclidean geometry using one specific dataset (FormalGeo-7k) and OpenAI's `ol` model. Generalizability to other geometric domains or more advanced datasets needs further validation. The choice of the `ol` model because other models produced too many syntax errors leaves a gap, since the `ol` model is less capable. It isn't clear that the other models couldn't be made to work with some pre-processing to make them less syntactically deficient.
    *   **Abstraction Schema:** The abstraction schema for identifying analogous problems seems relatively simple.  More sophisticated methods that capture deeper structural nuances or semantic relationships may further improve performance.
    *   **Closed LLM Dependency:**  The method relies on OpenAI's `ol` model, which limits its transparency and long-term reliability. The paper acknowledges this, but the reliance on a closed, proprietary LLM is a significant concern.
    *   **Limited Novelty with Core Theorem Proving:** The work's core novelty lies in how the LLM is guided and verified. It would be stronger if the method were capable of discovering novel proofs itself, rather than relying on proofs present in the analogous problems.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence research in neuro-symbolic AI, automated theorem proving, and the development of more reliable and trustworthy LLMs. The approach of combining analogical guidance and symbolic verification can be adapted to other formal domains and complex reasoning tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable and novel approach to improving LLM performance on mathematical proof generation. The experimental results are compelling and clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed neuro-symbolic framework. However, the limited scope of the evaluation, the reliance on a closed LLM, and relative simplicity of the abstraction method limit its overall impact and generalizability. The approach is more about making existing proofs accessible to the LLM rather than enabling the LLM to create novel proofs.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### MoMoE: Mixture of Moderation Experts Framework for AI-Assisted Online Governance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14483v1)
- **Authors**: Agam Goyal, Xianyang Zhan, Yilun Chen, Koustuv Saha, Eshwar Chandrasekharan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown great potential in flagging harmful content in online communities. Yet, existing approaches for moderation require a separate model for every community and are opaque in their decision-making, limiting real-world adoption. We introduce Mixture of Moderation Experts (MoMoE), a modular, cross-community framework that adds post-hoc explanations to scalable content moderation. MoMoE orchestrates four operators -- Allocate, Predict, Aggregate, Explain -- and is instantiated as seven community-specialized experts (MoMoE-Community) and five norm-violation experts (MoMoE-NormVio). On 30 unseen subreddits, the best variants obtain Micro-F1 scores of 0.72 and 0.67, respectively, matching or surpassing strong fine-tuned baselines while consistently producing concise and reliable explanations. Although community-specialized experts deliver the highest peak accuracy, norm-violation experts provide steadier performance across domains. These findings show that MoMoE yields scalable, transparent moderation without needing per-community fine-tuning. More broadly, they suggest that lightweight, explainable expert ensembles can guide future NLP and HCI research on trustworthy human-AI governance of online communities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MoMoE: Mixture of Moderation Experts Framework for AI-Assisted Online Governance":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MoMoE, a framework for AI-assisted content moderation designed to address limitations of existing LLM-based approaches. MoMoE operates as a mixture of experts (MoE), using a modular architecture consisting of four operators: Allocate, Predict, Aggregate, and Explain. The framework employs community-specific and norm-violation experts, fine-tuned small language models (SLMs).  It aims for scalable, transparent moderation without requiring per-community fine-tuning, providing post-hoc explanations for decisions.  Experiments on Reddit data demonstrate competitive performance against fine-tuned SLMs while generating concise and reliable explanations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of a Mixture of Experts (MoE) applied to content moderation, especially with the explicit goal of cross-community generalization *and* interpretable explanations, is the key novel contribution. Combining community-specific and norm-violation experts is also a sensible design choice for balancing adaptability and generalization. The modular architecture, separating allocation, prediction, aggregation, and explanation, allows for flexible experimentation and improvement of each component. The "Explain" operator adding post-hoc LLM based explanations is novel in the context of content moderation. Prior work typically focuses on accuracy or fairness, not the explicit generation of rationales for moderation decisions.

*   **Significance:**  The potential significance lies in addressing practical challenges hindering the real-world adoption of AI-based content moderation.  The modular approach reduces the need for extensive community-specific training data, making it easier to deploy in new or low-resource communities.  The emphasis on transparency through explanations directly tackles concerns about trust and accountability in AI-driven moderation.   By providing explanations, it enables moderators to understand how the system is working and identify potential biases or inconsistencies. If the approach proves robust and generalizable, it could significantly reduce the burden on human moderators and improve the consistency and fairness of content moderation across diverse online communities. The work helps bridge the gap between accurate machine judgements and how to enhance human oversight.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation against strong baselines, across multiple communities, and explores the impact of different operator choices.
    *   **Explainability:** The explicit focus on explainability is a major strength. The use of LLMs to generate rationales, and the qualitative assessment of their reliability, is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Well-defined architecture:** The modular design allows for independent improvement and experimentation with different components.
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of existing approaches and how MoMoE addresses them.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope of explanation evaluation:** While the authors claim 100% reliability in reflecting decision processes, a more in-depth evaluation of the *quality* and *usefulness* of the explanations from the perspective of human moderators is needed. Do these explanations actually improve trust, reduce workload, or lead to more effective moderation? Manual sampling of only four explanation samples is insufficient.
    *   **Reliance on prompted GPT-40 for explanation:** The explanation module relies on a closed-source LLM (GPT-40). This raises concerns about reproducibility, cost, and potential bias in the explanations themselves. Open-sourcing an effective "explain" operator or demonstrating its portability to an open model would be more compelling.
    *   **English-centric:** The evaluation is limited to English-speaking subreddits, which limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Simplified moderation scenario:** The evaluation focuses on simple comment removal. Real-world content moderation often involves more nuanced actions, such as warnings, temporary bans, or content edits.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in several areas:

    *   **Explainable AI for content moderation:** It could encourage more research on generating human-understandable explanations for moderation decisions.
    *   **Mixture of Experts for NLP:**  It could inspire the application of MoE architectures to other NLP tasks requiring both adaptability and generalization.
    *   **Human-AI collaboration in online governance:**  It could inform the design of tools that better support human moderators in their work.

*   **Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** because while the paper presents a novel and valuable architecture, a more thorough evaluation and a more robust explanation component are needed. The core concept of the MoMoE framework, the modular design, and the emphasis on explainability are significant contributions. However, the limited evaluation of explanation quality, the reliance on a closed-source LLM for explanations, and the simplified moderation scenario are notable weaknesses. While MoMoE shows promising results, the real-world impact will depend on the framework's ability to scale, handle complex moderation tasks, and provide explanations that truly empower human moderators. The high agreement on MoMoE's rationale is not sufficient for determining usefulness, meaning further human centered studies are needed.

### Reasoning Models Better Express Their Confidence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14489v1)
- **Authors**: Dongkeun Yoon, Seungone Kim, Sohee Yang, Sunkyoung Kim, Soyeon Kim, Yongil Kim, Eunbi Choi, Yireun Kim, Minjoon Seo
- **Abstract**: Despite their strengths, large language models (LLMs) often fail to communicate their confidence accurately, making it difficult to assess when they might be wrong and limiting their reliability. In this work, we demonstrate that reasoning models-LLMs that engage in extended chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning-exhibit superior performance not only in problem-solving but also in accurately expressing their confidence. Specifically, we benchmark six reasoning models across six datasets and find that they achieve strictly better confidence calibration than their non-reasoning counterparts in 33 out of the 36 settings. Our detailed analysis reveals that these gains in calibration stem from the slow thinking behaviors of reasoning models-such as exploring alternative approaches and backtracking-which enable them to adjust their confidence dynamically throughout their CoT, making it progressively more accurate. In particular, we find that reasoning models become increasingly better calibrated as their CoT unfolds, a trend not observed in non-reasoning models. Moreover, removing slow thinking behaviors from the CoT leads to a significant drop in calibration. Lastly, we show that these gains are not exclusive to reasoning models-non-reasoning models also benefit when guided to perform slow thinking via in-context learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the confidence calibration of reasoning models (LLMs employing chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning) compared to their non-reasoning counterparts.  The central finding is that reasoning models exhibit superior confidence calibration. This improvement is attributed to the "slow thinking" behaviors inherent in CoT, such as exploring alternative approaches and backtracking, which allow dynamic adjustments to confidence during the reasoning process. The study benchmarks models across various datasets and performs ablation studies to isolate the impact of slow thinking, even demonstrating that non-reasoning models benefit from being guided towards slow thinking via in-context learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic exploration of the relationship between reasoning (specifically CoT) and confidence calibration in LLMs. While prior work has addressed confidence in LLMs and CoT reasoning separately, this work connects the two in a rigorous, empirical manner.  It offers a novel perspective on why CoT might be beneficial beyond just task accuracy.  The discovery that slow thinking behaviors, rather than simply longer CoTs, are key to improved calibration is also a novel and valuable contribution. The ablation studies that analyze various reasoning behaviors are carefully designed, the finding that the *structure* of the CoT reasoning improves calibration is very helpful.
*   **Significance:** The findings are significant for several reasons. First, it provides a compelling justification for using reasoning models in scenarios where reliable confidence estimates are crucial (e.g., high-stakes decision-making). Second, it identifies specific CoT behaviors (exploring alternatives, backtracking) that contribute to better calibration, offering insights for improving LLM design and prompting strategies.  Third, the study addresses a critical weakness of LLMs – their tendency to be overconfident – and proposes a path toward more trustworthy and reliable models. The work has practical implications for how LLMs are deployed and trusted in real-world applications. The observation that non-reasoning models also benefit from slow thinking, when prompted through in-context examples, expands the applicability of findings.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmarking:**  The paper features extensive benchmarking across a diverse set of models and datasets, enhancing the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Rigorous Analysis:** The ablation studies are well-designed and executed, providing strong evidence for the role of slow thinking behaviors. The careful observation in their controlled experiment conditions allowed the conclusion of the importance of non-linear components.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the experimental setup, results, and analysis.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code, aiding reproducibility.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scale Limitations:** While the study uses 32B models, which is reasonable, the experiments may need to be re-evaluated as LLMs scale to trillions of parameters and are trained on more diverse data. Generalizability needs more consideration.
    *   **Instruction limitations:** There is limited diversity in instruction, which suggests findings in this paper may need to be replicated with larger prompt libraries.
    *   **Dataset Bias:** The datasets used in the experiments could introduce bias into the evaluation. A study on more diverse and real-world datasets could strengthen the claims.

**Justification of Score:**

Overall, the paper makes a valuable and novel contribution to the field of LLMs by establishing a clear link between reasoning and confidence calibration.  The rigorous analysis and well-supported conclusions provide valuable insights for improving the trustworthiness and reliability of LLMs.  The weaknesses identified above are primarily limitations related to scope and are not fundamental flaws. The findings have important implications for the development and deployment of LLMs in real-world applications.

### Enhanced Multimodal Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis by LLM-Generated Rationales
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14499v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Cao, Jiyi Li, Ziwei Yang, Renjie Zhou
- **Abstract**: There has been growing interest in Multimodal Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis (MABSA) in recent years. Existing methods predominantly rely on pre-trained small language models (SLMs) to collect information related to aspects and sentiments from both image and text, with an aim to align these two modalities. However, small SLMs possess limited capacity and knowledge, often resulting in inaccurate identification of meaning, aspects, sentiments, and their interconnections in textual and visual data. On the other hand, Large language models (LLMs) have shown exceptional capabilities in various tasks by effectively exploring fine-grained information in multimodal data. However, some studies indicate that LLMs still fall short compared to fine-tuned small models in the field of ABSA. Based on these findings, we propose a novel framework, termed LRSA, which combines the decision-making capabilities of SLMs with additional information provided by LLMs for MABSA. Specifically, we inject explanations generated by LLMs as rationales into SLMs and employ a dual cross-attention mechanism for enhancing feature interaction and fusion, thereby augmenting the SLMs' ability to identify aspects and sentiments. We evaluated our method using two baseline models, numerous experiments highlight the superiority of our approach on three widely-used benchmarks, indicating its generalizability and applicability to most pre-trained models for MABSA.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper, provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, assign a score between 1 and 10, and provide a thorough justification for the score.

**Summary:**

The paper "Enhanced Multimodal Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis by LLM-Generated Rationales" proposes a novel framework called LRSA for improving Multimodal Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis (MABSA). The core idea is to leverage the strengths of both small language models (SLMs) and large language models (LLMs). The framework uses LLMs to generate rationales (explanations) for understanding image-text pairs, which are then injected into SLMs to enhance their decision-making capabilities. A dual cross-attention mechanism is used within the SLMs to fuse the rationales with image and text features, aiming to improve the identification of aspects and sentiments. The authors evaluate their method on two benchmark datasets and demonstrate that LRSA outperforms baseline models, indicating its generalizability and applicability to MABSA tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The paper introduces a novel approach of combining LLMs for rationale generation and SLMs for decision making for MABSA. This is a departure from relying solely on either SLMs or attempting to directly fine-tune LLMs for ABSA.
*   **Addresses a Clear Problem:** The paper identifies a crucial limitation of current MABSA models: the limited knowledge and reasoning capabilities of SLMs when dealing with complex multimodal data. The use of LLM-generated rationales directly addresses this problem.
*   **Technical Contribution:** The dual cross-attention mechanism is a solid technical contribution that facilitates effective fusion of multimodal features and the LLM-generated rationales.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides empirical evidence to support its claims. The results on two benchmark datasets demonstrate the superiority of LRSA over existing methods. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of different components of the proposed framework.
*   **Ablation Studies & Prompt Engineering:** The ablation study effectively demonstrates the contribution of each component of the architecture. The careful consideration of different prompts for LLM-based rationale generation provides useful insights.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results are positive, the performance gains over the baselines, particularly in the context of increasingly large datasets and sophisticated models, are relatively incremental (around 1-2% F1 score increase).
*   **Reliance on LLM Rationales:** The entire framework is heavily reliant on the quality of the rationales generated by the LLMs. While the prompt engineering analysis is helpful, it also points to a potential fragility of the approach. Biases in the LLM or poorly designed prompts could negatively impact performance.
*   **Computational Cost:** Utilizing LLMs for rationale generation adds computational overhead, although the paper doesn't quantify this. A discussion of the trade-off between performance improvement and computational cost would be beneficial.
*   **Limited Scope:** The paper primarily focuses on two specific datasets (Twitter2015 and Twitter2017). Evaluating the framework on a wider range of datasets, including those with different characteristics, would enhance its generalizability.
*   **Justification of LLM Choice:** The selection of "Gemini-1.5 pro" isn't strongly justified beyond just stating its multimodal capabilities. While this is a reasonable choice, discussing alternative models, their pros/cons, and why they weren't selected would make the paper stronger.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant because it demonstrates a promising approach to enhance MABSA by combining the strengths of SLMs and LLMs. The LRSA framework offers a practical way to incorporate external knowledge and reasoning capabilities into existing MABSA models. The insights from prompt engineering also provide valuable guidance for effectively leveraging LLMs in this domain. The paper makes a valuable contribution by exploring how LLMs can be used, not as direct replacements for existing models, but as helpful tools to improve their performance.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to improving MABSA by leveraging LLM-generated rationales. The technical contributions are solid, and the empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework. However, the incremental performance gains, the reliance on LLM rationales, and the limited scope of the evaluation prevent it from achieving a higher score.

The paper has a clear novelty and importance, addressing a critical gap in the existing MABSA research. The idea of combining both SLMs and LLMs together provides new research directions for sentiment analysis.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning to Integrate Diffusion ODEs by Averaging the Derivatives
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14502v1)
- **Authors**: Wenze Liu, Xiangyu Yue
- **Abstract**: To accelerate diffusion model inference, numerical solvers perform poorly at extremely small steps, while distillation techniques often introduce complexity and instability. This work presents an intermediate strategy, balancing performance and cost, by learning ODE integration using loss functions derived from the derivative-integral relationship, inspired by Monte Carlo integration and Picard iteration. From a geometric perspective, the losses operate by gradually extending the tangent to the secant, thus are named as secant losses. The secant losses can rapidly convert (via fine-tuning or distillation) a pretrained diffusion model into its secant version. In our experiments, the secant version of EDM achieves a $10$-step FID of $2.14$ on CIFAR-10, while the secant version of SiT-XL/2 attains a $4$-step FID of $2.27$ and an $8$-step FID of $1.96$ on ImageNet-$256\times256$. Code will be available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Learning to Integrate Diffusion ODEs by Averaging the Derivatives":

**Summary:**
The paper introduces a novel approach to accelerate diffusion model inference by learning ODE integration using "secant losses". This approach sits between computationally expensive distillation techniques and fast samplers that often degrade rapidly with very few steps.  The method leverages the derivative-integral relationship inspired by Monte Carlo integration and Picard iteration. The core idea is to learn the secant (the average rate of change between two points) instead of directly modeling the tangent (instantaneous rate of change). The secant losses enable converting pre-trained diffusion models into secant versions via fine-tuning or distillation. Experiments show that secant versions achieve good FID scores with significantly fewer steps compared to standard numerical solvers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel perspective on ODE integration within the context of diffusion models. Framing the diffusion model as learning a "tangent" and then proposing to learn the "secant" provides a helpful geometric intuition. The use of Monte Carlo integration and Picard iteration as inspiration for the loss functions is also a unique angle. While other methods have tackled faster inference, the specific approach of directly learning to approximate the integral using secant losses is a meaningful contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a key bottleneck of diffusion models: slow inference. Accelerating inference without sacrificing image quality is highly valuable.  While other distillation and faster sampling methods exist, this paper offers a different trade-off, potentially sidestepping some issues such as training instability or excessive computational overhead common in other approaches. The experimental results demonstrating reasonable performance with very few steps, especially on ImageNet, suggest a practical benefit.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear geometric intuition:** The tangent/secant framing simplifies understanding the approach.
    *   **Stable training:** The paper claims better training stability compared to consistency models, potentially due to avoiding explicit differentiation.
    *   **Efficient distillation/fine-tuning:** The method allows for rapid conversion of pre-trained models.
    *   **Good performance with few steps:** Achieves impressive FID scores on both CIFAR-10 and ImageNet with a small number of function evaluations.
    *   **Thorough evaluation:** The paper includes evaluations of various choices for loss weight, noise sampling strategies etc.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on classifier-free guidance (CFG):** The ImageNet results rely heavily on CFG, limiting the applicability and generalizability of the results. More analysis on the theoretical link between CFG and this work is needed.
    *   **Limited step improvement compared to some other methods:** While faster than diffusion directly, some distillation methods can achieve one or two-step generation, albeit with more computational cost or stability issues. The improvement might not be groundbreaking if ultimate step count reduction is the primary goal.
    *   **The performance in other datasets/tasks has not been proved:** The generality of this work need to be proved in text-to-image generation/video generation.
    *   **Not a groundbreaking result:** The paper does not present any groundbreaking result, its incremental improvement of existing methods instead.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in diffusion model acceleration, potentially leading to new techniques that combine secant learning with other approaches. It might also inspire new ways to think about approximating integrals in generative models.

*   **Rigor of rationale:** The paper could improve on the rationale section for certain choices.
*   **Theoretical contributions:** The theoretical contributions are limited.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and well-executed method that achieves decent results in a critical problem in diffusion models, faster generation with limited steps. This approach is robust than consistency models because they bypasses explicit differentiation. However, the overall performance highly depends on classifier-free guidance, potentially limiting the impact to other tasks. Further, it's an incremental improvement from the existing methods, making it a potentially more novel and less groundbreaking result.

### ModRWKV: Transformer Multimodality in Linear Time
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14505v1)
- **Authors**: Jiale Kang, Ziyin Yue, Qingyu Yin, Jiang Rui, Weile Li, Zening Lu, Zhouran Ji
- **Abstract**: Currently, most multimodal studies are based on large language models (LLMs) with quadratic-complexity Transformer architectures. While linear models like RNNs enjoy low inference costs, their application has been largely limited to the text-only modality. This work explores the capabilities of modern RNN architectures in multimodal contexts. We propose ModRWKV-a decoupled multimodal framework built upon the RWKV7 architecture as its LLM backbone-which achieves multi-source information fusion through dynamically adaptable heterogeneous modality encoders. We designed the multimodal modules in ModRWKV with an extremely lightweight architecture and, through extensive experiments, identified a configuration that achieves an optimal balance between performance and computational efficiency. ModRWKV leverages the pretrained weights of the RWKV7 LLM for initialization, which significantly accelerates multimodal training. Comparative experiments with different pretrained checkpoints further demonstrate that such initialization plays a crucial role in enhancing the model's ability to understand multimodal signals. Supported by extensive experiments, we conclude that modern RNN architectures present a viable alternative to Transformers in the domain of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Furthermore, we identify the optimal configuration of the ModRWKV architecture through systematic exploration.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ModRWKV, a novel multimodal framework built upon the RWKV7 architecture, a modern RNN architecture.  ModRWKV aims to extend the capabilities of RNNs to cross-modal tasks, traditionally dominated by Transformer-based models.  The framework utilizes a modular design, employing modality-specific encoders (vision, audio, time series) coupled with lightweight adapter layers to align features with the RWKV7 backbone.  The paper explores different encoder choices, adapter configurations, and sequence compression techniques to optimize performance and computational efficiency.  The authors perform extensive experiments across various multimodal benchmarks to demonstrate the effectiveness of ModRWKV and its potential as a viable alternative to Transformers in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The experiments focus on vision understanding, audio recognition, and time series forecasting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in adapting a modern RNN architecture (RWKV7) to the multimodal domain.  While RNNs are known for their low inference costs, their application in multimodal settings has been relatively limited.  The introduction of modality-specific encoders and lightweight adapters is a standard practice in multimodal learning, making this aspect less innovative in itself. The choice of RWKV7 as the base architecture and its successful application to multimodal tasks *is* a significant contribution. The ablations and design choices for the adapters and conv1d compression offer useful insights.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work hinges on the potential of linear complexity models to offer more efficient alternatives to Transformers, especially for resource-constrained environments.  If ModRWKV can achieve comparable performance to Transformer-based MLLMs with significantly lower computational costs, it could have a substantial impact. The demonstration that RNNs can perform well, even rivaling transformers in some areas, challenges the current dominant paradigm. However, the performance gap to the best Transformer-based MLLMs is likely still significant, meaning that this might be a worthwhile area for further research but not a paradigm shift.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation across a diverse set of multimodal benchmarks, covering vision, audio, and time series data.
    *   **Modular Design:** The plug-and-play design of the encoder framework facilitates scalability and integration of different modalities.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation experiments provide valuable insights into the optimal configuration of the ModRWKV architecture, balancing performance and computational efficiency.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper is accompanied by code, enhancing reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Performance Comparison:** While the paper highlights the benefits of RNNs, it could benefit from more direct, head-to-head comparisons against larger Transformer-based MLLMs on the most challenging benchmarks. A comprehensive comparison of FLOPs and inference latency is crucial for demonstrating a true advantage in computational efficiency. The work should also analyze whether the improvements are statistically significant.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** The encoder/adapter choices are relatively standard. The incremental benefit of each component should be carefully examined to demonstrate why the chosen solution is superior.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Fusion Strategies:** The paper primarily focuses on concatenating features from different modalities. Exploring more sophisticated fusion mechanisms could potentially improve performance further. The use of fixed or frozen pretrained weights for the encoders is a possible bottleneck.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to stimulate further research on applying modern RNN architectures to multimodal learning. The insights gained from the encoder selection, adapter design, and sequence compression techniques can be valuable to other researchers in the field. While not revolutionary, it provides a practical example and insights that can be used by the research community.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid, but incremental contribution. While the individual components (encoders, adapters) aren't revolutionary, the successful integration of RWKV7 within a multimodal framework is valuable. The thorough evaluation across multiple modalities and ablations strengthens the findings. However, the lack of strong comparisons to state-of-the-art transformer-based models and the incremental nature of the improvements limit its overall impact.

### Latent Flow Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14513v1)
- **Authors**: Yen-Chen Wu, Feng-Ting Liao, Meng-Hsi Chen, Pei-Chen Ho, Farhang Nabiei, Da-shan Shiu
- **Abstract**: Transformers, the standard implementation for large language models (LLMs), typically consist of tens to hundreds of discrete layers. While more layers can lead to better performance, this approach has been challenged as far from efficient, especially given the superiority of continuous layers demonstrated by diffusion and flow-based models for image generation. We propose the Latent Flow Transformer (LFT), which replaces a block of layers with a single learned transport operator trained via flow matching, offering significant compression while maintaining compatibility with the original architecture. Additionally, we address the limitations of existing flow-based methods in \textit{preserving coupling} by introducing the Flow Walking (FW) algorithm. On the Pythia-410M model, LFT trained with flow matching compresses 6 of 24 layers and outperforms directly skipping 2 layers (KL Divergence of LM logits at 0.407 vs. 0.529), demonstrating the feasibility of this design. When trained with FW, LFT further distills 12 layers into one while reducing the KL to 0.736 surpassing that from skipping 3 layers (0.932), significantly narrowing the gap between autoregressive and flow-based generation paradigms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Latent Flow Transformer paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Latent Flow Transformer (LFT), a novel architecture for efficient language modeling. LFT replaces blocks of transformer layers with a single, learned transport operator trained using flow matching. This aims to achieve compression while maintaining compatibility with the original transformer architecture. To address limitations of flow matching in preserving coupling, the paper proposes Flow Walking (FW), a scalable algorithm for learning latent transport using numerical integration.  Experiments on the Pythia-410M model demonstrate that LFT, particularly when trained with FW, can significantly compress the model (distilling several layers into one) while maintaining or even improving performance compared to simple layer skipping. The paper introduces an interpretable metric, the Recoupling Ratio, to guide the selection of layers suitable for replacement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of replacing transformer blocks with a learned flow-based transport operator is novel. The introduction of the Recoupling Ratio as a heuristic for layer selection contributes to the understanding of which layers are amenable to this type of compression. Flow Walking, as a technique for addressing flow path proximity in the latent space, represents a valuable addition to flow-matching methods.
*   **Significance:** If successfully scaled and validated, LFT has the potential to significantly reduce the computational and memory footprint of large language models. This could lower the barrier to entry for deploying and fine-tuning these models. The combination of continuous and discrete representation within one model will inspire more work to bridge autoregressive generation and flow-based generation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies a problem (inefficiency of large transformer models) and proposes a plausible solution grounded in existing work (flow matching, image generation).
    *   The empirical results on the Pythia-410M model, while limited in scale, demonstrate the feasibility and potential benefits of the LFT architecture.
    *   The Recoupling Ratio offers an interpretable approach to layer selection for replacement.
    *   The Flow Walking algorithm provides a solution to the limitation of the standard flow matching.
    *   Clear presentation of concepts and experimental methodology.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are conducted on a relatively small model (Pythia-410M). The benefits of LFT need to be demonstrated on larger, more state-of-the-art models to assess its practical applicability.
    *   While LFT reduces parameter, the latency of computing Flow-based transfer may increase. The paper hasn't evaluated it.
    *   The effectiveness of LFT is heavily dependent on the performance of the flow matching training. The plateau is likely to be still there if scaling up the model.
    *   The explanation of the theoretical underpinning of Flow Walking (FW) could be more elaborate.
    *   The evaluation metrics used, while standard, do not fully capture the nuances of language generation quality.
    *   The novelty, though present, is incremental. The core idea relies on leveraging existing flow-matching techniques in a new context (language modeling), rather than introducing entirely new theoretical foundations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence research in:
    *   Model compression for large language models.
    *   The application of continuous dynamics and flow-based techniques to discrete sequence modeling.
    *   Methods for understanding and optimizing the internal structure of transformer models.

*   **Concerns**
    *   Even though the authors introduce FW algorithm to stabilize the training in latent space. The experiments need to evaluate if this algorithm can alleviate mode collapse and increase the quality of the generated data.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to transformer compression with promising initial results. However, the limited scale of the experiments and the dependence on existing flow-matching techniques temper its immediate impact. The potential for significant efficiency gains and the introduction of the Recoupling Ratio justify a positive assessment. However, further research is needed to address the scalability concerns and fully demonstrate its benefits on larger models and diverse tasks.
